# Game Roadmap — Road to Release 1

> **Status: active living checklist.** Design direction lives in
> [Game Guidelines](GAME_GUIDELINES.md); this file owns task order and
> status. Reorganized on 2026-09-29 around **Release 1**. The former
> `BETA_PLAN.md` was folded into this file and the guidelines, then removed.

## Release 1 (v0.1) — The Target

Release 1 is a free web build of **Chapters 1 to 3** (owner, 2026-10-15; it
was Chapters 1 and 2). For two chapters the target was about an hour of play
from the title screen to an end-of-Chapter-2 card: a first-time player takes
**45–75 minutes**, timed in the blind playtest (11.1). Release 1's content
now ends when the Crystal Colossus falls, but the game goes on: the player
explores the first three chapters' regions, part of the West village and the
Wilds with the new powers, and the game says that more areas come in later
releases (owner, 2026-10-16,
[question 58](story/07-decisions-archive.md#contradictions-left-after-the-review-2026-10-15);
12.5). Its play time is set in 12.2. In it, a player can:

- start from a title screen, continue an autosave, pause, change settings, and
  wake at their last bed after a defeat;
- play a short **prologue** before Chapter 1 (owner, 2026-10-16,
  [question 97](story/07-decisions-archive.md#contradictions-left-after-the-review-2026-10-15);
  the [prologue design](design/2026-10-16-prologue.md); 5.18–5.24): the
  slime is born at the Wellspring, learns to move and to gulp, sees Yoi fall
  to four worms, saves him by throwing things and learns the Spear from him,
  then opens a barred gate into Slimeshire, where Plop meets it at the town's
  edge;
- finish Chapter 1 in Slimeshire Meadow (playable today; Planned: the Roll
  from young Burr in the West village, and Burr freed in Fatty's Lair), then
  Chapter 2 in Gloop Forest (a new enemy, the Forge and a second boss), then
  Chapter 3 in the Crystal Caverns (a
  [sketch](story/chapters/chapter-3-crystal-caverns.md) with the smith's mine
  camp and a third boss, Milestone 12);
- restore a Slimeshire building, the Forge, which brews the potions in
  Release 1 ([question 51](story/07-decisions-archive.md#contradictions-left-after-the-review-2026-10-15));
  a building is restored when a Pearl comes home
  ([question 60](story/07-decisions-archive.md#contradictions-left-after-the-review-2026-10-15)),
  and Pearl 2 relights the Forge
  ([question 79](story/07-decisions-archive.md#contradictions-left-after-the-review-2026-10-15));
  since the seventh round every Pearl goes home to the Wellspring, and its
  power flows into the town (question 110); since the eighth round the relit
  Forge brews the strong potions only it knows, and campfires make the
  simple ones (question 120, 8.5); *since 2026-11-20 (owner) Pearl 2 raises
  the Workshop, Pip's place, and Pearl 3 lights the Forge (6, 8.5)*;
- grow stronger through skins, shapes, heart quarters and story-unlocked
  abilities (no XP or levels; shapes replace crafted gear since 2026-10-15);
  since the eighth round each boss also gives a full heart, and potions, the
  well and each boss raise max energy (questions 17 and 19; 5.3, 5.6);
- win three skins from the Pearls (Heavy, Silk and Feral:
  [question 56](story/07-decisions-archive.md#contradictions-left-after-the-review-2026-10-15));
  since 2026-10-17 each Pearl is found inside its dungeon, before the boss,
  and the dungeon's second half teaches its skin
  ([the 2026-10-17 decisions](#the-2026-10-17-decisions)),
  and grab and throw with the **Gulp**
  ([the 2026-10-15 decisions](#the-2026-10-15-decisions));
- craft potions and food from what is picked up and dropped, the only
  crafting in Release 1
  ([question 52](story/07-decisions-archive.md#contradictions-left-after-the-review-2026-10-15));
  before the Forge is relit, potions are found or bought and Chapter 1's
  simple recipes are made at a campfire, and the West village shop sells
  potions and food ingredients
  ([questions 88 and 89](story/07-decisions-archive.md#contradictions-left-after-the-review-2026-10-15));
  since the sixth round potions and food are both crafted at any campfire,
  and campfires stand by the Wellspring, in the town and at each camp
  (questions 66 and 104, 5.17);
- earn a bag and **eight powers besides the Gulp**, about three a chapter,
  each with the item ceremony
  ([question 94](story/07-decisions-archive.md#contradictions-left-after-the-review-2026-10-15);
  [the fourth and fifth rounds](#the-fourth-and-fifth-rounds-questions-84-to-94)):
  - Chapter 1: the Spear (Yoi, in the prologue since the sixth round, 5.12),
    the Roll (young Burr, 5.13) and the Heavy skin (Pearl 1, 5.9);
  - Chapter 2: the Goo Bomb, the chapter's key tool (where it comes from is
    [question 95](story/07-decisions-archive.md#contradictions-left-after-the-review-2026-10-15);
    *answered in the sixth round: Tom, at the end of his maze, 8.13*),
    and the Silk skin (Pearl 2);
  - Chapter 3: the Lantern (the smith, 12.3), the Feral skin (Pearl 3) and
    the Bow (Burr, from the Matron's thread, 12.6; *since 2026-11-20 Pip's
    bow rune, from 5 wood, the thread and the smith's crystal*).

  Release 1 has one bag, Plop's 10-slot bag, and the vault and the 20-slot
  bag wait
  ([the 2026-10-02 decisions](#the-2026-10-02-decisions);
  [questions 53 and 74](story/07-decisions-archive.md#contradictions-left-after-the-review-2026-10-15)).
  With the War Axe in the next release the Spear is its only shape
  ([question 96](story/07-decisions-archive.md#contradictions-left-after-the-review-2026-10-15),
  open; *answered in the sixth round: E stays hidden until a second shape is
  learned, and the Axe opens the next release*);
- hear music and ambience in every area and feel every hit.

**Not in Release 1:** the Kitchen, farming, hats, harvesting and crafted
weapons and tools (2026-10-15), manuscript pieces and Slime Forge evolutions
("upgrades of skins and abilities come later in the game", owner,
2026-10-15), skins 4 to 8 (the Swim skin among them since 2026-10-16, so the
deep-water secrets are come-back secrets; the Swim form's mechanics become
its power, question 75; since the ninth round the Swim, Polar, Sand, Cloud
and Lava skins, question 32), the vault, the 20-slot bag, the charged roll and the
Roll's enemy push (2026-10-16), the Hammer shape (Chapter 5 or 6, question
51), the **War Axe** shape and **Stretch Lash** (2026-10-16, question 94:
their locks, such as webs, vines and trees, Sunny's basket and the Lash's
other uses, become come-back secrets), the
unconnected `icege`/`emberleef`/`hot` maps, the mobile app, and custom editor
tooling. They wait in
[After Release 1](#after-release-1--idea-parking-lot). The Crystal Caverns,
a locked teaser until 2026-10-15, are now Chapter 3. The Bow was on this list
until Burr was given it as his thanks; whether it is in Release 1 was
[question 85](story/07-decisions-archive.md#contradictions-left-after-the-review-2026-10-15).
*Answered 2026-10-16: it is, in Chapter 3 (questions 85 and 94, 12.6).*

**No target date.** Release 1 ships when it is ready. By choice, milestones
are ordered but not sized or dated (decided 2026-09-29).

### Rules That Keep Release 1 Shippable

- **New ideas go to the [parking lot](#after-release-1--idea-parking-lot)**,
  never straight into a Release 1 milestone. An idea moves in only by replacing
  work of the same size.
- **Tooling is not progress unless a player can see the result.** Levels and
  scenes are built in the Godot editor; Scene Studio was retired with the Phaser
  app (2026-10-05).
- **Every milestone lists its art and audio.** Placeholders are fine while
  building; a milestone is `[x]` only when its assets are final or explicitly
  accepted as final. All new art follows the
  [art style guide](assets/visual-style-guide.md) and is generated with
  Magnific following the [Magnific MCP guide](assets/magnific-mcp-guide.md),
  then packed and registered through [docs/assets/](assets/README.md).
- **New features prove they are fun first.** A new mechanic (Gulp, the slime
  trail, a new enemy behavior) is built in the dev-only `playground` map
  (3.7). It enters chapter content only after the user accepts it there.
- **Testing:** at the end of each milestone (not after every task), Claude
  plays the milestone's player proofs once in the in-app browser and reports
  what works. The user does the final acceptance. Players who have never seen
  the game test twice: after the game shell (4.9) and before release (11.1).
- **A task is `[x]` only when a fresh save can do it in the running game**,
  without debug grants.
- **Finish milestones in order.** Two exceptions: the rough Gulp prototype
  (7.0) runs right after the playground (3.7), and Milestone 9 (game feel) may
  run alongside 6–8. No task's check depends on a later milestone; work that
  needs later content lives in the milestone that builds that content.
  Milestone 12 (Chapter 3, added 2026-10-15) comes after 8 and before 10.

### The 2026-10-08 Decisions

On 2026-10-08 the owner removed the jump and redesigned Gulp.

- **The jump is removed from the game** (done 2026-10-08). It was stronger than
  the other abilities (its landing check ignores placed objects, so it hopped
  over walls and gates), it did not fit the game, it was needed only for the
  Heavy landing on cracked ground, and it misbehaved around elevation. Its
  logic stays: the `jump` action has no key (Space is free), no HUD slot or
  Controls row shows it, nothing in the story teaches it, and ledge drops
  still use its flight. Tasks: 4.10, 5.2, 5.9, 7.4.
- **Fatty One Eye's Pearl grants Squash Slam** instead of the jump: his fight
  is a leap that lands hard. Until the Pearls exist (5.9), finishing The
  One-Eyed Guardian teaches the slam, and The Matron's Nest no longer does
  (done 2026-10-08; 8.7).
- **Cracked ground breaks under a Heavy Squash Slam**, not a Heavy jump
  landing (done 2026-10-08). A Heavy slime standing on it hears it creak and,
  once the slam is learned, is told to slam it. Tasks: 7.4, 8.9.
- **Gulp is redesigned around essences**, replacing the armors (the design is
  in [Gulp](GAME_GUIDELINES.md#signature-mechanic-gulp)).
  - Holding Q sucks in an essence or a light object (pots and dozens of other
    things for every kind of place), and Q throws it. A thrown thing breaks by
    what it is made of. Pots are fine as long as they are the game's own, not
    copies of Zelda's (owner, 2026-10-09).
  - Swallow, learned from Tom in Chapter 2, turns a held essence into its form.
  - Some enemies drop essences and some maps hold them.
  - A hit pops the form out as a blob.
  - Tasks: 7.5, 7.6, 8.12 (the Clod Stomper, the stone essence's enemy) and
    8.13 (Tom and the Swallow lesson).
- **The Roll replaces the Dodge** on key 2: it spins up in place, then dashes
  straight on while held, like Zelda's Pegasus Boots, and bonks back off
  walls. Built 2026-10-09, waiting for the owner's feel. Task 7.7.
- **New keys** (changed by the owner on 2026-10-09, applied with 7.5): Q sucks
  and throws; 1 swallows; 2 rolls; Stretch Lash, Squash Slam and Teleport
  move to 3, 4 and 5; interact stays on right-click (W in the keys-only
  scheme). The 2026-10-08 plan (R swallows, Space
  interacts) was dropped. Task 4.10.

Several of these changed on 2026-10-15 (below): the Pearls grant skins,
Squash Slam is removed, the Goo Bomb breaks cracked ground, and Swallow,
essences and these number keys go with the old system.

### The 2026-10-15 Decisions

On 2026-10-15 the owner moved the slime's powers to skins, shapes and
abilities ([Direction](story/01-direction.md)), and the same day answered the
review of that direction
([questions 40 to 51](story/07-decisions-archive.md#review-of-the-2026-10-15-direction)).
Like the 2026-10-02 decisions, they came in by the owner's choice, not through
the parking-lot rule. The rework mode, merged into main the same day, already
builds the shapes, the Ability Box, the Goo Bomb, the Lantern, heavy plates and
blocks, energy shards, the slow energy refill and the quick roll
([BODY_SHAPES.md](godot/BODY_SHAPES.md)); the classic game is still the
Gulp-and-essences build. Where a task depends on a question that is still
open, it says "Open:" and links the question; nothing here decides one.

#### The third round (questions 68 to 83)

Later on 2026-10-16 the owner answered questions 68 to 83
([open questions](story/07-decisions-archive.md#contradictions-left-after-the-review-2026-10-15)).
Like the decisions before them, these came in by the owner's choice. None is
built yet. Questions 84 to 86 follow from them and are open; a task that
depends on one says "Open:" and links it. *They were answered later the same
day ([the fourth and fifth rounds](#the-fourth-and-fifth-rounds-questions-84-to-94)).*

| Decision | Roadmap tasks |
|---|---|
| A climbable wall looks different from the others: it has vines and irregularities. The slime climbs up and down, and a fall costs nothing (question 68) | 7.8 (changed), 12.1. Open: vines are also the War Axe's lock (question 86). *Answered in the fourth round: thick roots and rocky handholds* |
| The Colossus climbs out of reach and throws some of his minions down; killed, they can drop energy shards (question 69) | 12.4 (changed), 5.10 |
| Chapter 1's slab bridges cross the void and spikes, and lava waits for a fire region. The slabs, the spikes and the lava share one hazard script, and the void works like the elevation's holes. A slab falls a set time after it is touched (in `game-constants.json`), tuned so quick rolls in a row cross it and walking never does (questions 70, 71) | 5.14 (changed) |

The **Heavy Roll** owns old dead or built things that lie or lean in the way (grey leaning old trees, rotten trunks across a path, old fences, plank barricades, rotten mine timbers, loose rocks, rubble heaps). | **Plop** meets the slime at the town's edge as it comes out of the forest, gives it the 10-slot bag and its first tips, with the exclamation mark, and later sends it to Burr. He no longer greets it at the Wellspring (question 100) | 5.24 (new), 5.7 and 5.17 (changed) |

what a bell opens stays open for good.*

#### The ninth round (questions 32, 118, 121, 123 and 136)

Later on 2026-10-16 the owner accepted the table of the later bosses and
their skins (question 32), took the proposal of question 136, which followed
from the eighth round, and took the proposals of questions 118, 121 and 123,
already built that way
([decisions archive](story/07-decisions-archive.md#decisions-by-date)). Like
the decisions before them, these came in by the owner's choice. The open
questions left are 3, 4, 9, 10, 116 and 117.

| Decision | Roadmap tasks |
|---|---|
| **The later skins** ([question 32](story/07-decisions-archive.md#skins-shapes-and-abilities-2026-10-15)): one skin per vassal, each a new way through the world that opens come-back secrets in the places before it. **Old Gullet**, the catfish (Sticky Swamp), gives the **Swim skin**: deep water. **Old Rimepaw**, the polar bear (a working name; Frostpeak), who slides across the frozen lake on his belly and cracks the floes with a slam, gives the **Polar skin**: the slime is sure-footed, and cold no longer hurts. Without it the slime slips on ice and cannot change direction, which closes many places and secrets; with it, ice and every slippery surface (oil and others, in other maps too) are walked without trouble. On ice its Roll becomes a **belly slide**, long, fast and steerable, across frozen lakes and through ice-race secrets. Its look: a white, fluffy coat with a blue tint, and padded paws that show on ice. **Sandmaw**, the great sand worm and Gorge's worm general (the Desert), gives the **Sand skin**: it burrows through soft sand, under sand walls and through quicksand. **Gale**, the storm roc (the Northern ruins), gives the **Cloud skin**: it floats across gaps from a ledge and rides updrafts. **Cinder**, the magma salamander (Volcano Ridge's slopes), gives the **Lava skin**: it walks on lava and through fire, and as the last skin it opens the way into Gorge's crater. **Gorge** is the ninth boss, with no skin. The acid skin stays a spare. The order of the later regions is still question 10 | Release 1 target (above), the parking lot |
| **Bells stay open:** what a rung bell opens stays open for good, so a bell is a lock solved once ([question 136](story/07-decisions-archive.md#open-after-the-eighth-round-2026-10-16)) | 5.22 (changed) |
| **As built:** quitting during the prologue starts it again from the birth (question 118); the dev-only worlds keep their spawn areas, as testbeds (question 121); the skeletons' bone pile stays (question 123) | 5.18, 5.25 and 12.7 (changed) |

None of the later skins is in Release 1. The order of these tasks is not set.

#### The tenth round (questions 18 and 20)

Later on 2026-10-16 the owner settled the loose ends the eighth round's docs
pass found
([decisions archive](story/07-decisions-archive.md#decisions-by-date)). Like
the decisions before them, these came in by the owner's choice.

| Decision | Roadmap tasks |
|---|---|
| **The Gloop Cavern stays** ([question 18](story/07-decisions-archive.md#progression), changed): told that a small `gloop-cavern` world is built behind the cracked clearing's sinkhole, the owner kept it as a small secret cave: the Goo Bomb's come-back secret, holding one of Gloop Forest's four heart quarters, with no four of its own. Its old coffer question stays closed | 8.9, 7.10, 5.3 and 5.7 (changed) |
| **Grass** is cut by the Spear and, later, the War Axe; it drops energy, health and sometimes coins. Grass tufts never look like the Axe's bushes | 5.10 (changed) |
| **Wooden clappers** are the worm army's alarms: anything that hits one (a pot, an arrow, a Spear jab, the slime bumping it) sets it off and calls the nearby camp. A trap to avoid or use, never a lock: bells are the locks | 5.22 (changed), 5.25 |
| **The refill pause:** spending pauses the 1-a-second energy refill for about a second, tuned in play ([question 20](story/07-decisions-archive.md#energy-rework-details)) | 5.6 (changed) |

The order of these tasks is not set.

### The 2026-10-17 Decisions

On 2026-10-17 the owner moved each dungeon's Pearl inside it, before its
boss, as Zelda's dungeon item
([decisions archive](story/07-decisions-archive.md#decisions-by-date)). It is
his own idea ("it changes the pace of the game", owner), and like the
decisions before it, it came in by the owner's choice. The three dungeons built the same
day (5.15, 8.14, 12.1) still have the old order, the boss and then his
Pearl; their rework is 12.8, with its
[plan](design/2026-10-17-pearl-mid-dungeon.md).

| Decision | Roadmap tasks |
|---|---|
| **The Pearl inside the dungeon:** a boss cannot swallow his Pearl whole, so he hides it in his dungeon and keeps it close to draw on its power. The slime finds it there, before the boss | 5.9 and 7.8 (changed), 12.8 (new) |
| **The boss notices:** taking the Pearl enrages him (his strength stays the same), and he seals the dungeon's exits: the only way out is to beat him | 5.9, 5.15, 8.14 and 12.4 (changed), 12.8 |
| **The skin's half:** the dungeon's second half teaches the Pearl's skin, and the boss fight is the skin's exam. This replaces the level design guide's "each boss's way out uses the skin it gives" and, for the Heavy and Feral skins, the first locks of question 132 | 7.8, 5.15, 8.7, 8.14, 12.4 and 12.5 (changed), 12.8 |
| **Fainting or quitting after the Pearl** resumes at the dungeon's entrance, inside, with the doors to the outside still sealed until the boss falls (question 109's resume, changed) | 5.15 (changed), 12.8 |
| **The Pearls still go home** to the Wellspring afterwards (question 110 stands) | 5.17 (nothing changes) |

The rework (12.8) follows its plan; the order of the other tasks is not set.

### The 2026-11-20 Decisions

On 2026-11-20 the owner put two ideas straight into Release 1
([decisions archive](story/07-decisions-archive.md#decisions-by-date)). Like
the rounds before, they came in by the owner's choice, not through the
parking-lot rule.

| Decision | Roadmap tasks |
|---|---|
| **A falling-slab maze in Chapter 3:** the Crystal Caverns' outside becomes a maze over several levels of mountains and cliffs, with islands and crystal caves that lead to other parts of it, monsters and secrets, and the smith found in a mine cave on its main road; the mine's gate early on that road, so the trip to Pip for the Bow stays short; a maze of long falling slabs, crossed with the Roll; under it the void or ground that hurts ("the cliff could be void or damaged areas", owner). Its long runs are the kit we have, crossed only by the Charged Roll, as the plank bridge to C5's lake island is, and they stand on the main way too, so Chapter 3 needs the four Roll Fragments | 12.9 (new) |
| **Compact tree walls:** a tree wall shows whole trees only along its front, and behind them only treetops, a closed canopy, as many games do, so a forest band reads as a wall that cannot be passed. The owner's reference: one solid mass of big, round, overlapping canopies with no ground between them, and roots and trunk bases only along its foot. Today's one-tree-per-cell painting leaves gaps that look open where the collision still stops the slime; single trees keep today's art | 12.10 (new) |

Milestone G (2026-10-04) moves the game from Phaser to Godot. Its
[plan](GODOT_MIGRATION.md) owns the phases. Done 2026-10-05: the owner played
Chapter 1 through in Godot and the Phaser app was removed; the Release 1
milestones continue in Godot (entries dated before the cutover describe the
Phaser build).

Why this order: 3 closes the work already half done. 4 gives every later
playtest a real start and end. 5 comes before new content so Chapter 2 is
balanced once, against gear. 6 and 7 build the systems Chapter 2 uses. 12
(added 2026-10-15) brings Chapter 3 after Chapter 2. 10 and 11 turn the build
into a release.

## How To Use The Checklist

- `[ ]` not started, `[~]` in progress, `[x]` verified in the running game,
  `[—]` dropped (keep the line and say why).
- After each milestone, do a short fresh-save playtest and note what became
  possible.
- Unresolved design choices go to the guidelines'
  [open questions](GAME_GUIDELINES.md#open-questions), not into silent rules.

Task tile format:

```md
### [ ] 6.1 — Short task name

- Build: the smallest implementation or content change.
- Player proof: what the player can see or do afterward.
- Done when: the concrete acceptance check passes.
```

Each milestone ends with an **Assets** list: what can be reused and which new
art or audio must be made. See [docs/assets/](assets/README.md) for sizes,
packing, and registration.

## 3. Finish Homes, Interiors, And Audio

The home stays in Slimeshire (Level 1) and never moves. Other maps can have
their own homes with their own interiors, linked by doors. The last bed slept
in is where the player wakes.

### [x] 3.1 — Doors link to doors (was 4.1)

- Done: `game.door` ScriptNodes name a `targetDoorId` and own an `arrival`
  child, so leaving a house puts the player in front of the door they used.
  Level 1 doors lead to `slime-home` and `mushroom-home` and back; a test checks
  that every door targets an existing door with an arrival.

### [x] 3.2 — First interior visual kit (was 4.2)

- Done: `wood-floor` terrain plus one `object.interior-<category>-*` scene per
  atlas sprite (842 scenes, generated from `scripts/interiors/interior_catalog.py`).
  `world.slime-home` and `world.mushroom-home` are furnished rooms.

### [x] 3.3 — Enter, move, and leave (was 4.4)

- Done: F near a door travels through the queued navigation path; interior
  worlds use a fixed camera that fits the room; a bobbing F badge marks the
  door, chest, or NPC in range.

### [x] 3.4 — Beds are home (was 3.3), verified 2026-09-29

- Done: sleeping saves `world.respawnPoint` with the bed's identity (`bedId`:
  the instance's persistence key, or `placed-furniture:<id>` for a placed bed).
  Defeat wakes the player at that bed on its own map; if the bed's world no
  longer has it (`features/rest/RespawnDestination.ts` checks the scene
  catalog), the point is cleared and the player wakes at the Level 1 spawn.
  Played headless: sleep in the Slime Home, die in Level 1, wake in the bed;
  the bed survives a reload; a removed bed falls back to Level 1.
  `pnpm test:progression` covers the lookup.

### [x] 3.5 — Interior collision and interaction (was 4.5), verified 2026-09-29

- Done: `game.bed` scripts on sleepable beds (F dozes, sleeps, and restores
  `rest.sleepHpRegenPerSec` HP/s; input or damage wakes the player). A
  flood-fill of where the player's body can stand shows both rooms closed:
  no footprint reaches wall art. Beds, tables, hearths, the alchemy bench and
  the water basin got deeper footprints (`FOOTPRINT_SHARES` in
  `scripts/interiors/interior_catalog.py`), so the player can no longer walk
  into the hearth alcove or across tabletops. Depth and the F badge were
  checked from the north, south, east and west of each large piece.

### [x] 3.6 — Persist placed furniture (was 4.6), verified 2026-09-29

- Done: the workbench is placed from the inventory with a grid-snapped
  preview (R variant, Esc cancel), saved per map as `placedFurniture`, and
  picked up with G. Played headless: one bench in Level 1 and one in the Slime
  Home each stay exactly once through leaving, returning, and a reload;
  picking one up and placing it again leaves one record.

### [x] 3.7 — The playground map, verified 2026-09-29

- Done: `world.playground` (40 × 28 tiles) has a resource grove, an empty
  cobble puzzle yard, an enemy pen (worm brawlers, a worm archer, a slime
  spider) and a chest of stone gear, materials and potions by the spawn. It
  opens with `?map=playground`. `src/game/content/scenes/devOnlyWorlds.ts` is
  the dev-only world list; the scene content plugin drops those worlds from
  production builds (a build contains no `world.playground`). 10.2 adds the
  other test maps to the same list. Dev-only worlds have no legacy
  `.map.json`, so a named save made in one cannot be loaded back.

Interior authoring in Scene Studio (was 4.3) is not a separate task: the next
interior, the Gloop Forest hut, is built in 8.8 with Scene Studio as it is
today, and only real blockers get fixed.

- Player proof: Slimeshire sounds alive and props can be heard as you approach.

### [~] 3.10 — Audio polish (was A.6)

- Build: duck music while paused, crossfade on `area.enter`, switch to boss

### [x] 3.11 — Steps, water and the silent actions (Sound Picker rounds 3 and 4), applied 2026-10-06

## 4. Game Shell

Built 2026-09-29 as `features/shell/` (one `MenuSurface` base, one authored
`ui.*` scene per menu, composed by `GameShell`); `pnpm test:shell` covers the
menus and hints. Played headless end to end: fresh profile → title (Continue
and Load disabled) → New Game → play → Esc → Save to Slot 1 → Quit to Title
(Continue and Load enabled) → Load Slot 1 (same position) → defeat → "Defeated
by Worm Brawler" → wake. The Development Tools panel (with its Save, Load, and
Reset) only exists in `pnpm dev`. Placeholder presentation until the assets
below exist.

### [x] 4.1 — Title screen, verified 2026-09-29

- Done: the first world of a page load is a title screen over a paused,
  empty Slimeshire that drifts slowly (`WorldScene` title mode; nothing is
  autosaved behind it). New Game (asks first when an autosave exists; save
  slots are kept), Continue (`SaveSystem.continueLatest`: the autosave, else
  the newest slot), Load, Settings, Credits, and the version. Run handoffs
  (travel, load, reset) and dev `?map=` previews skip it.

- Build: New Game, Continue (newest save or recovery autosave), Load,
  Settings, Credits, and the version number. A slow camera pan over Slimeshire
  can serve as the background.
- Player proof: the game starts like a game.
- Done when: every button works on a fresh browser profile and with saves.

### [x] 4.2 — Pause menu, verified 2026-09-29

- Done: Esc with nothing open pauses and shows Resume, Journal, Inventory,
  Map, Settings, Save, and Quit to Title (which saves, fades out, and reloads
  to the title). Settings and Save open on top of it; Esc closes the topmost
  window first. The sound settings are part of Settings.

- Build: Escape with no other surface open pauses and shows Resume, Journal,
  Inventory, Map, Settings, Save, and Quit to Title, routed through the
  existing `ModalStack`. The sound settings become a Settings tab.
- Done when: pause state, Escape order, and resume are correct from every
  surface.

### [x] 4.3 — Player-facing saves, verified 2026-09-29

- Done: three save slots (named saves "Slot 1"–"Slot 3") from Save in the
  pause menu, and Load from the title or the game-over screen; overwriting a
  slot asks first. The P.5 matrix stays covered by `pnpm test:persistence`
  plus the menu round trip above.
- Since 2026-10-16 ([the sixth round](#the-sixth-round-questions-62-to-67-and-95-to-107);
  [question 62](story/07-decisions-archive.md#contradictions-left-after-the-review-2026-10-15)):
  like Zelda, the slime saves everywhere except inside dungeons, and there
  are no save points (5.15 makes Save unavailable in Fatty's Lair). What
  happens on quitting or dying inside a dungeon is
  [question 109](story/07-decisions-archive.md#contradictions-left-after-the-review-2026-10-15)
  (open; the proposal: the slime resumes at the dungeon's entrance).
- Since the [seventh round](#the-seventh-round-questions-108-to-114)
  (questions 109 and 113): quitting inside a dungeon resumes at its
  entrance, and the dungeon keeps what the slime did there (small keys,
  opened doors, the map, the big key); the autosave waits until the slime is
  out (5.15). The prologue cannot be saved either (5.18).

- Build: move Save, Load, and Reset Run out of Development Tools into the pause
  menu and title screen, and hide Development Tools in production builds.
- Player proof: a player can save, quit, and continue without developer UI.
- Done when: the P.5 persistence matrix still passes through the new menus.

### [x] 4.4 — Game over, verified 2026-09-29

- Done: after the defeat animation, "Defeated" names the attacker from the
  last hit ("Defeated by Worm Brawler") and the time played, with "Wake at

  and crafting that fade after first use.

- Done when: on a fresh save each of the six hints appears once, disappears
  after the player performs that action, and never returns on reload.

- Built 2026-10-01 after the owner played Chapter 2, waiting for the owner:
  - **Tutorial:** the first time the menu opens, a pointer under the tab strip
    names the Bag, Crafting, Journal and Map tabs until one is clicked. A new

    asks to open the bag, the Crafting tab, the Journal and the map, to sprint
    and to pause; its objectives are the new `use-control` kind and the
    tracker adds each key. Stone Tools asks to switch tools on the belt. Four
    more first-time hints (switch weapons, sprint, map, pause), and quest text
    no longer names unbound keys ("(C)", "(F)", "U · Quest book").
  - **Belt:** four slots, and the weapon in hand always swings (tools no longer
    swap in by themselves, which lost fights against enemies behind a tree). A
    tree or rock the weapon cannot harvest says where the right tool is.
  - **Bag:** the weapon belt is always shown at the top with each slot's
    weapon and an IN HAND tag; weapons drag from the bag onto a slot (the
    weapon there goes back to the bag); bigger cells with names, a status line
    (in hand / on belt slot N / in the bag), bigger buttons.
  - **Fangs:** level 1 has no orb-weavers; its Meadow spiders now use the
    yellow spider sheet (`character.enemy.meadow-spider`), and a tinted enemy
    keeps its tint after a hit flash. A drop that does not fit in the bag says
    so instead of vanishing.
  - **Load:** the Load window (title, pause menu, defeat screen) lists the
    autosave, shows why an unreadable slot cannot load, fits stale quest
    progress instead of failing the load (that left a blank screen), and
    returns to the title with the reason if a save still cannot be installed.
    The autosave no longer records a defeated slime.
  - **Dev panel:** "Weapon hitboxes" draws the slime's live swing (yellow
    open, green hit, red refused) and "Hurtboxes" where the slime, enemies and
    bosses take damage, bosses labelled with the weapons they accept. Ticking
    a box no longer scrolls the page (the game was cut in half).
  - **Fatty:** his eye hurtbox follows the drawn eye in every clip (it missed
    the landing and recovery punish windows), every spear reaches it (the Iron
    Spear did not), hits flash and show damage, and he heals to full after a
    minute with the slime outside his arena (the Matron too).
- Played headless (Brave): the bag drag, belt click, tab pointer, Slime
  Basics progress, a stale-quest save loading, the defeat-screen load, the
  overlays and a spear hit on Fatty.
- Planned 2026-10-02: Slime Basics drops its Sprint step and the sprint hint
  goes, because sprinting becomes a mid-game ability (7.7); its bag step comes
  after Elder Plop's bag (5.7).
- Second list, built 2026-10-01:
  - **Loot on the ground:** an enemy's item drops scatter around where it
    fell and are picked up by walking over them, like resource piles; they
    count for quests on pickup and persist until picked up (bag drops' store,
    `origin: 'loot'`). Picking your own bag drop back up no longer counts
    for quests or berry coins, and a regrown tree or rock gives its piles
    again (their old "collected" records were never cleared).
  - **Mossy's quests:** Beyond the Verdant Gate (v2) asks for 5 orb-weavers
    and 3 fangs together; A Harder Pick (v2) starts at the pickaxe. Saves
    reconcile.
  - **Gloop Forest:** tree walls on the west, east and south edges (openings
    at the level-1 entry and the crystal corridor), a deep-water band on the
    north; the Matron's nest is ringed by trees with one web-draped entrance
    to the west, its arena carpeted with webs, egg sacs and victim cocoons.
    Webs, hanging and ground cocoons (motion-only idle loops) dress every
    orb-weaver thicket, the silk nook and level-1's Webwood and south meadow.
    Art: `sheet.props.web-decor.4x3` (Magnific, 11 pieces).
  - **Windows:** bag cells are one size, crafting shows the item, its
    description and a materials list with have / need, the Quest Book shows
    the quest's name and state on top, and every window's buttons are centred.

### [~] 4.9 — First fresh-eyes playtest

- Owner playtest, 2026-09-29. Findings and what changed (2026-09-30):
  - Crafting a placeable item needed close → inventory → place: crafting one
    now closes the popup and starts placing it at once (Esc keeps it in the
    bag).
  - Missing materials were easy to miss: Craft stays pressable and answers
    "Missing: 15 Wood." in red; short recipes show a red edge and "Missing 15
    Wood" in the list; the details mark each short material.
  - The first worm camp was too hard: it now holds worm swordsmen (slower,
    telegraphed), and Worm Trouble asks for three of them (definition v2;
    older saves keep their progress, clamped to the new target).
  - Quests needed a WoW-style pointer: clicking the quest tracker shows a gold
    arrow with the distance to the next step (giver, turn-in NPC, camp, boss,
    exit, tree or rock, workbench), a marker over it once on screen, and a pin
    on the minimap (`features/quests/QuestWaypoint.ts`).
  - Fatty's camp was cramped and cheesable: his clearing is now an open
    7-tile circle with no props, walls or trees inside, his arena and
    activation circles sit just inside it (so he never leaps over hedges), the
    chest waits at the north rim, and the maze has no enemy spawns, so no
    worm joins his fight (`scripts/maps/build-level-1.mjs`).
  - Credits read "Created by Daniel, Isa and Dany6".
- Owner feedback, 2026-09-30, and what changed:
  - A web crossed with the Sticky form could trap a slime whose form wore off
    on the far side: the web now tears open for good when the Sticky slime
    pushes through (remembered per placement).
  - Cracked ground broke too easily: the Heavy form now has to land a jump on
    it; standing on it only makes it creak, with a hint. (Since 2026-10-08 a
    Heavy Squash Slam breaks it: 7.4.)
  - Enemies could be killed from just outside their pursue area, where they
    stood passive: camp enemies now keep a territory
    (`enemies/ai/Territory.ts`): sight blocked by walls, houses and trees, a
    hit always alerts them, a leash (distance from home plus how far the
    player is out of reach) decides how far they chase, a lost player is
    searched for 3 s where last seen, and they walk home healing. Played
    headless: a worm engaged at 150 px in sight, searched then went home when
    the slime hid behind the ruins, and healed to full after giving up.
  - The controls felt odd: attacking by clicking while walking with the
    arrows, and many keys that each do one thing. Planned as 4.10.
- Open: a second new player.

- Build: two people who have never seen the game play Chapter 1 from the
  title screen while you watch without helping. Note where they get stuck and
  time each run.
- Player proof: Chapter 2 is built on what new players actually did, not on
  guesses.
- Done when: both runs have written notes and a time recorded under this task,
  every blocker they hit is fixed or has its own task, and lessons that affect
  Chapter 2 are carried into the outline (8.1).

### [~] 4.10 — Simple mouse-and-keyboard controls

- Plan: [Simple controls design](archive/phaser/superpowers/specs/2026-09-30-simple-controls-design.md),
  agreed 2026-09-30 after the owner feedback in 4.9.
- Built 2026-09-30, waiting for the owner's playtest:
  - every control comes from one binding table
    (`features/player/PlayerInputActions.ts`), and so does every key label;
  - Dodge is a learned ability (Stone Tools), and Jump was on Space (the jump
    was removed on 2026-10-08, and Space is free);
  - the belt has three slots on the mouse wheel, and tools pick themselves
    (both changed by the 2026-10-01 playtest, 4.11: four slots, no automatic
    tools);
  - right click interacts with what the pointer is on, and holding it picks
    up placed furniture;
  - E opens the menu with tabs (since 2026-10-08 B does, J opens the journal
    and E is the weapon scroll: 4.12);
  - cheats are dev-panel buttons.
  - Two headless playtests passed 34 of 34 checks. See
    [As Built](archive/phaser/superpowers/specs/2026-09-30-simple-controls-design.md#as-built-2026-09-30).
- Open:
  - the owner picks the attack direction (pointer or facing; the dev panel's
    "Controls test" switch);
  - the key and mouse glyph art;
  - planned 2026-10-02: Shift no longer sprints from the start; a mid-game
    roll replaces sprinting (7.7).
- Decided 2026-10-09 (owner), applied with 7.5: Q sucks and throws; 1
  swallows; 2 rolls (the Roll, built 2026-10-09, 7.7); 3 Stretch Lash, 4 Squash Slam,
  5 Teleport; interact stays on right-click (W in the keys-only scheme).
- Changed 2026-10-15 ([the 2026-10-15 decisions](#the-2026-10-15-decisions)):
  the rework mode's keys become the only ones when the old system goes
  (7.11): left click attacks with the shape, E switches shapes, Q is the
  mouth, F uses the ability chosen in the bag (which pauses the game), Space
  rolls, and right click interacts. The number keys for Swallow, Stretch
  Lash, Squash Slam and Teleport go.
- Changed 2026-10-16 ([the sixth round](#the-sixth-round-questions-62-to-67-and-95-to-107);
  [question 96](story/07-decisions-archive.md#contradictions-left-after-the-review-2026-10-15)):
  the Spear is Release 1's only shape, so E stays hidden until a second
  shape is learned (7.11).
- Build: the left hand on WASD, the right hand on the mouse.
  - Left click attacks, and the right tool is picked automatically for trees
    and rocks. Whether attacks aim at the pointer or the facing is under test,
    with a switch.
  - Right click interacts with whatever the pointer is on.
  - Space is Jump (removed 2026-10-08; Space is free). 1 is Dodge, a roll of
    0.5 s toward the pointer (4 directions) that Stone Tools now teaches (the
    Roll on 2 since 2026-10-09, 7.7).
    Holding Shift sprints.
  - Q is the mouth (Gulp), and 2–4 are the other abilities.
  - The mouse wheel switches between up to 3 equipped weapons.
  - E opens one menu with tabs.
  - One binding table supplies every key label, and key presses expire after
    150 ms.
- Player proof: a new player finishes Chapter 1 without taking their right
  hand off the mouse.
- Done when: the plan's "Done When" checks pass on a fresh save, and the owner
  accepts the feel in the playground and in a Chapter 1 run.

### [~] 4.13 — The item ceremony

- Built 2026-10-09 for abilities, then the same day for permanent items and
  Goo Hearts ("implement the ceremony so i can test in level 1"), waiting for
  the owner. The owner asked for the slime to raise what it learned, "or
  something original but exciting". Code: `game/ui/ceremony/ability_ceremony.gd`
  and `ceremony_stage.gd`.
  - The world pauses (pause reason `ceremony`) and dims around the slime,
    like a spotlight. The slime squashes and stretches up, and the fanfare
    plays (cue `Ceremony`).
  - A glossy goo bubble carrying the thing's icon (an ability's HUD console
    `ability-<id>.webp`, an item's bag icon, the heart's own picture) wells
    out of the top of the slime and floats above it, wobbling, with 14
    turning light rays and sparkles.
  - A card slides up: what it is ("New ability", "New weapon", "New tool" for
    a weapon that harvests, "Key item", "Goo Heart"), the name, one line of
    what it does, and how to use it (an ability's `AbilityDefinitions.CEREMONY`;
    a weapon's "It goes on your belt: swing it with <attack>", a tool adding
    what it harvests; a key's "it opens a lock somewhere"), with the keys from
    the 4.10 binding table. A key counts after 1.6 s, and "Press any key"
    shows at 1.8 s.
  - Any key: the bubble sinks back into the slime, which gulps it down (cue
    `GulpCatch`, a squash, a burst of sparkles). The dim lifts and the game
    goes on.
  - It waits while a window is open (a crafted tool plays once the crafting
    window closes) and queues when several things come at once.
  - `game/quests/quest_notifications.gd` plays it for every active ability
    `RunState` learns while main's `ability_ceremonies` is on (the default).
    The test runner turns it off, so tests still see the old banner. The
    passive Goo Trail keeps its banner.
  - Items: the first time a weapon or tool or a key (`RunState`
    `PERMANENT_ITEM_CATEGORIES`) comes into the bag, by any way in (a pickup,
    a chest, a craft, a reward), `RunState` remembers it in the save
    (`story.celebrated_item_ids`) and emits `permanent_item_gained`, and
    `quest_notifications.gd` plays its ceremony. It plays once per item and
    never again after save and load. Quiet grants (the start weapon, the
    playground's kit, `?arsenal`) count without one, and so does whatever an
    older save already holds.
  - Every Goo Heart plays it too ("+10 max HP", `goo_heart.gd`); the floating
    text stays.
  - Tests: `godot/tests/test_ceremony.gd` (`pnpm test:godot -- --filter=test_ceremony`).
  - `godot/tools/render_ceremony.gd` renders it in the running playground for
    tuning.
- Not done: bags (5.7), heart quarters (5.3), energy upgrades, the lift
  upgrades and story items (they end in it when they are built), the smaller
  recipe sting and the owner's acceptance. The fanfare is Sound Picker round
  5's option A (the owner accepted the defaults on 2026-10-09). *Since the
  [eighth round](#the-eighth-round-questions-11-to-38-and-115-to-135): the
  lift upgrades are gone (question 28), and each boss's full heart ends in it
  too (question 17, 5.3).*
- Changed 2026-10-15 ([the 2026-10-15 decisions](#the-2026-10-15-decisions)):
  the skins (5.9, 7.8) and the shapes (5.12) end in it too. The crafted
  weapons and tools, Squash Slam and Swallow go (5.11, 7.11), and hammering
  was dropped (6.5), so the checks below that name the Stone Axe and the
  crafted weapons change with them.
- Decided 2026-10-02 ([Direction](story/01-direction.md#decisions-of-2026-10-02-planned-not-built),
  "Getting an item"): the game pauses, the item rises out of the slime with
  light rays and a fanfare, and a card shows the name, one line of text and
  how to use it. Permanent items get the full ceremony the first time. New
  recipes get a smaller sting, and materials get nothing. New player
  animations are welcome when they improve the game.
- Today: a learned ability, the first of each weapon, tool and key, and every
  Goo Heart play the ceremony above (the passive Goo Trail shows its banner);
  everything else shows floating text. In level 1: the Stone Axe and Stone
  Pickaxe from the workbench, the Roll from Elder Plop, the Verdant Key at
  Fatty's camp and the lakeside Goo Heart.
- Build: one reusable ceremony. Permanent things are those in
  [Direction](story/01-direction.md#the-rule-behind-every-chapter): weapons
  and tools, abilities, the lift upgrades, bags and vaults, hearts, heart quarters and
  energy upgrades, keys and story items. The [unlock map](story/05-unlock-map.md)'s
  Ceremony column is the checklist. The card's "how to use it" takes its key
  labels from the 4.10 binding table. Wire it to what exists today (crafted
  weapons and tools, the Roll, Squash Slam, Stretch Lash, Swallow from Tom
  (8.13), the green key, Goo Hearts); the bags (5.7), the vault (5.8), the
  Pearls (5.9) and hammering (6.5) end in it when they are built.
- Player proof: earning something permanent is a moment, like a Zelda chest.
- Done when: on a fresh save the first Stone Axe, the Roll and the green key
  each play the full ceremony once and never again after save and load; a
  learned recipe plays the smaller sting; picking up wood plays neither; the
  game stays paused under the card and resumes cleanly.

**Milestone 4 complete when:** boot → title → new game → play → defeat →
wake → pause → settings → save → quit → title → continue works end to end,
two new players have played Chapter 1 (4.9), the new controls are in (4.10),
and the bottom console HUD and the item ceremony are in (4.12, 4.13, added
2026-10-07). The loop works (headless, 2026-09-29); open: 4.9, 4.10, 4.12,
4.13 and the assets below.

## 5. Gear-Based Progression

Decided 2026-09-29: the player gets stronger through gear, not experience.
XP, levels, and perks are removed. See
[Progression](GAME_GUIDELINES.md#progression-gear-not-levels).

Built 2026-09-29 (5.1–5.5). The player has flat base stats
(`character.player.stats` in `game-constants.json`), Goo Hearts raise max HP,
and quests teach abilities the way they teach recipes. The work is verified in
the running game with placeholder art and sound; the assets below are open.

Reopened 2026-10-07 for the [2026-10-02 decisions](#the-2026-10-02-decisions):
heart quarters (5.3), an energy cost for every ability (5.6), bags (5.7), the
vault and its house quest (5.8), and the bosses' Pearls (5.9).

Changed 2026-10-15 ([the 2026-10-15 decisions](#the-2026-10-15-decisions)):
crafted gear gives way to skins, shapes and abilities. The Pearls grant skins
(5.9), energy refills slowly and comes back in more ways (5.6, 5.10), Squash
Slam is removed (5.11), and Old Veteran Yoi teaches the Spear (5.12).

Changed 2026-10-16 ([the 2026-10-16 decisions](#the-2026-10-16-decisions)):
Release 1 has one bag and no vault (5.7, 5.8). Chapter 1 gains young Burr's
Roll (5.13), the falling slabs and their hazards (5.14), Fatty's Lair's fast
gate and Burr's cell (5.15) and the West village's part of Release 1 (5.16),
and the journal takes the quests' place (5.17). In the
[third round](#the-third-round-questions-68-to-83) the same day: Plop's
10-slot bag is the one bag (5.7), Chapter 1's slabs cross the void and spikes
and fall a set time after they are touched (5.14), Burr's thanks is the Bow
(5.15), the West village's part is a small town (5.16), and an NPC with a tip
shows the exclamation mark (5.17). In the
[fourth and fifth rounds](#the-fourth-and-fifth-rounds-questions-84-to-94):
the West village shop sells potions and food ingredients (5.16), potions
before the Forge are found or made at a campfire (5.17), Pearl 1 restores
Slimeshire's well, which refills energy (5.10, 5.17), and Burr's Bow moves to
Chapter 3 (5.15, 12.6). In the
[sixth round](#the-sixth-round-questions-62-to-67-and-95-to-107): a prologue comes before
Chapter 1 (5.18 to 5.24), where Yoi teaches the Spear, and his hideout
becomes his home (5.12); Plop meets the slime at the town's edge (5.7, 5.24);
Burr builds the Bow from the Matron's thread (5.15, 12.6); and after Fatty
the slime is sent home, where the well gives a full refill, and campfires
make Chapter 1's recipes (5.10, 5.17). Later the same day: no saving inside
dungeons and no save points (5.10, 5.15), and potions and food at a
campfire and whole manuscripts in chests (5.17). In the
[seventh round](#the-seventh-round-questions-108-to-114): the manuscripts
teach campfire recipes and lore (5.17); a dungeon resumes at its entrance
(5.15); every Pearl goes home to the Wellspring, and its power brings the
well back (5.9, 5.10, 5.17), whose refill grows back; the Spear's upgrades
wait for after Release 1 (5.12); the prologue takes B4's south edge, cannot
be saved and shows the HUD after the birth (5.18), and the West village's
town takes B4's north and A4 (5.16); the Weaver Hollow's big chest holds a
heart quarter (5.3); Spear-only bells join the barred gate as the Spear's
locks (5.22); and enemies in the world are placed, not spawned (5.25). In the
[eighth round](#the-eighth-round-questions-11-to-38-and-115-to-135): each
boss also gives a full heart (5.3, 5.9); potions, the well and bosses raise
max energy, basic attacks and the Roll stay free, and enemies and grass drop
energy, health and sometimes coins (5.6, 5.10); the house quest and the
vault's open points close (5.8); bells become a ranged lock, rung by a thrown
pot or an arrow, the first near the town's edge (5.22, 5.12); the Roll's
first lock is a rickety plank in Burr's yard (5.13); Burr's cell lies on
the lair's main path, and the lair's way out is the Heavy skin's first lock
(5.15); and lore pages are read again on the journal's "War journal" page
(5.17). In the [tenth round](#the-tenth-round-questions-18-and-20): spending pauses the refill (5.6), the Spear
cuts grass (5.10), and wooden clappers are alarms, not locks (5.22).

Changed 2026-10-17 ([the 2026-10-17 decisions](#the-2026-10-17-decisions)):
each Pearl is found inside its dungeon, before its boss, and taking it seals
the exits until he falls (5.9); Fatty's Lair's second half teaches the Heavy
skin, and Fatty is its exam (5.15). The built lair is reworked with the other
two dungeons in 12.8.

### [x] 5.4 — Rebalance Chapter 1 without levels, verified 2026-09-29

- Done: base stats are the old level-2 values (attack 12, defense 3), with 100
  max HP and 100 energy. Chapter 1 quests give about 410 XP, so a typical
  player ended the chapter at level 2. Measured in the running game with no
  crits (axes and pickaxes do not hurt worms), before the change at level 2
  and after it on a fresh run:

  | Check | Before (level 2) | After (fresh run) |
  |---|---|---|
  | Wooden spear hits to defeat a worm brawler (55 HP) | 8 | 8 |
  | Stone spear hits to defeat a worm brawler | 7 | 7 |
  | Wooden spear hits to defeat Fatty One Eye (140 HP) | 20 | 20 |
  | Stone spear hits to defeat Fatty One Eye | 16 | 16 |
  | Worm brawler hits the player survives | 2 | 2 |
  | Worm swordsman hits the player survives | 3 | 2 (3 with one Goo Heart) |
  | Worm archer arrows the player survives | 5 | 5 (6 with two Goo Hearts) |

  Level 1 for reference: 10 and 7 swings per brawler, 24 and 18 on Fatty,
  survives 1 brawler hit.

- Build: set base stats so a fresh run with stone gear matches today's typical
  end-of-Chapter-1 player. Fatty One Eye and the worms keep their current
  numbers and behavior.
- Done when: before 5.1, record how many hits each starter weapon needs to
  defeat a worm brawler and Fatty One Eye, and how many worm hits the player
  survives; after the change, a fresh Chapter 1 run with the same gear matches
  each number within one hit, with no debug grants.

### [x] 5.5 — Resources respawn, verified 2026-09-29

- Done: a depleted tree, stone node or ore node records
  `respawnReadyAtEpochMs` (now + `resources.respawnMs`, provisionally 10
  minutes) in the map's saved progress. When its map loads after that time
  and its dropped piles are collected, it grows back full
  (`features/resources/ResourceRespawn.ts`); older saves start the timer on
  first load, so nothing returns early. Only scripts that opt in regrow
  (`ResourceNodeScript.regrows`). Played headless: a chopped tree stayed gone
  through a trip into the Slime Home before its time and stood again at full
  health after it. `pnpm test:progression` covers the rules.
- Changed 2026-10-15 ([the 2026-10-15 decisions](#the-2026-10-15-decisions)):
  Release 1 has no harvesting: the Axe and the Hammer give no wood, stone or
  iron. The respawn goes with harvesting when the old system goes (7.11).

- Build: harvested trees and stone nodes (and later iron nodes) come back after
  a long respawn time set in `game-constants.json`, tracked per map in the
  saved progress like Fatty's respawn timer. Loose starter piles and quest
  rewards still keep a fresh save softlock-free.
- Player proof: returning to a harvested area later finds it grown back.
- Done when: a harvested node respawns after its timer across map changes and
  save/load, and never respawns early.

### [~] 5.6 — Every ability costs energy

- Decided 2026-10-02 ([Direction](story/01-direction.md#decisions-of-2026-10-02-planned-not-built),
  "Energy"): every ability costs energy, some per use (Dodge, Squash Slam),
  others over time (rolling, 7.7). Energy is not too easy to recover, so
  cooldowns get shorter. Max energy grows over the game.
- Changed 2026-10-15 ([the 2026-10-15 decisions](#the-2026-10-15-decisions);
  [question 40](story/07-decisions-archive.md#review-of-the-2026-10-15-direction)):
  every ability still costs energy, per use (the Goo Bomb 20, a Lantern flame
  10, Stretch Lash, Teleport) or over time (the held Roll), but the quick Roll
  is free. Skins and shapes are free, except a shape's charged attack (15 to
  25). Energy refills by itself, slowly: "energy can recover but really slow,
  like 1 per second; in addition we will add more ways to recover energy"
  (owner). The more ways are 5.10. Squash Slam goes (5.11). Max energy still
  grows over the game.
- Built in the rework mode (2026-10-15): the refill of 1 a second
  (`character.player.rework.energyRegenPerSecond`; none while rolling), the
  new abilities' and the charges' costs in `game-constants.json`
  (`abilities.*`, `shapes`), and the free quick roll (`quickRollEnergy` 0).
  The classic game still refills 8 a second, and Stretch Lash's and
  Teleport's costs are still literals in `ability_definitions.gd`.
- Today: only Stretch Lash (20), Squash Slam (30) and Teleport (35) cost
  energy per use; the Roll costs nothing to start, and only its held part
  drains 12 per second (`rollEnergyPerSecond`, 7.7; energy does not refill
  while rolling). Max energy is 100 and refills 8 per second
  (`character.player.stats` in `game-constants.json`). The costs and cooldowns
  are literals in `game/player/abilities/ability_definitions.gd`.
- Build: an energy cost for every ability, per use or per second; the costs
  and cooldowns move into `game-constants.json`; a slower refill and shorter
  cooldowns; max energy that can grow (saved with the run, like the max HP
  that hearts add). The Roll (7.7) uses the same costs; Gulp forms cost no
  energy (2026-10-08, 7.5).
  Tune in the playground first.
- Open: the refill pause, free basic attacks, tiny cooldowns, a minimum to
  start rolling, and energy drops
  ([questions 20–24](story/07-decisions-archive.md#energy-rework-details)); the
  item that raises max energy and where it is found
  ([question 19](story/07-decisions-archive.md#progression)). *Answered in
  the eighth round (below), except the refill pause (question 20).*
- Changed 2026-10-16, eighth round
  ([the eighth round](#the-eighth-round-questions-11-to-38-and-115-to-135);
  questions 19 and 21 to 24):
  - **Basic attacks stay free**, so the slime is never helpless, and the
    **tiny cooldowns stay** as an anti-spam guard (questions 21 and 22).
  - **Rolling is free:** the quick Roll needs no energy to start, as built
    (`quickRollEnergy` 0); only the held part drains (question 23).
  - **Enemies and grass drop** energy and health refills, and sometimes
    coins (question 24): the drops are 5.10's.
  - **Max energy** grows from potions, the well and defeating a boss
    (question 19; 5.9, 5.10).
  - Build (added): max energy raised for good by those three, saved with the
    run like the max HP that hearts add, each raise in `game-constants.json`.
  - Not decided yet: which potions raise it, by how much each raise is, and
    when the well gives its raise. Whether the refill pauses after spending
    is still question 20's. *Answered in the tenth round (below).*
  - Done when (added): basic attacks cost nothing; a quick roll starts at 0
    energy; each raise adds its amount to max energy once and survives save
    and load.
- Changed 2026-10-16, tenth round
  ([tenth round](#the-tenth-round-questions-18-and-20);
  [question 20](story/07-decisions-archive.md#energy-rework-details)):
  spending pauses the 1-a-second refill for about a second, tuned in play.
  - Build (added): the pause after each spend, its length in
    `game-constants.json`, tried in the playground.
  - Done when (added): after a spend the refill waits about a second, then
    goes on at 1 a second; the owner accepts the length.
- Player proof: the slime has to choose when to dodge, lash or slam.
- Done when: in the playground each learned ability spends its configured

  [eighth round](#the-eighth-round-questions-11-to-38-and-115-to-135) as
  answered before: Release 1 has no quests, so there is no house quest, and
  an NPC hands the home over (question 11); more vaults, and whether they
  share one storage, are decided with the vault in a later release
  (questions 12 and 38). Which house is not decided yet.*
- Depends on: 4.13 and 5.7.
- Player proof: the slime has a home to rest in and somewhere to keep what the
  bag cannot hold.
- Done when: on a fresh save the quest follows Stone Tools; sleeping in the
  house's bed completes its step and sets the respawn point; the vault's
  ceremony plays once; items and a weapon dragged into the vault and back
  stay where they were put through a map change and save and load; a second
  vault in the playground shows the same contents.

### [ ] 5.9 — A boss's Pearl grants its skin

- Changed 2026-10-15 ([the 2026-10-15 decisions](#the-2026-10-15-decisions)):
  each Pearl grants a **skin** (7.8), not an ability; the task was "A boss's
  Pearl grants its ability". Pearl 1, Fatty One Eye's, grants Heavy; Pearl 2,
  the Matron's, Silk; Pearl 3, the Crystal Colossus's, Swim (Chapter 3,
  Milestone 12; *changed 2026-10-16: Swim comes from a later Pearl, after
  Release 1, and Pearl 3 grants the Feral skin,
  [question 56](story/07-decisions-archive.md#contradictions-left-after-the-review-2026-10-15)*).
  The skin stays when the Pearl goes home to the Wellspring. Since
  2026-10-16 (question 60; 5.17) a won Pearl fills its slot in the journal,
  and a Pearl that comes home restores a Slimeshire building.
  Squash Slam is removed (5.11), and the Matron's Pearl no longer gives
  Stretch Lash. Bosses live in their dungeons: once the slime enters a boss's
  chamber it cannot leave until the boss is beaten, and a beaten boss never
  comes back.
  - Build: a Pearl that a beaten boss leaves; taking it plays the full
    ceremony (4.13) and grants the boss's skin for good. Fatty One Eye leaves
    Pearl 1 and the Matron Pearl 2; Pearl 3 comes with Chapter 3 (12.1). The
    chamber holds the slime until its boss is beaten, and the boss never comes
    back.
  - Open: the premise around the Pearls
    ([question 2](story/07-decisions-archive.md#story)). Answered since
    2026-10-02: the Wellspring is its own new map east of level-1
    (question 5; *changed 2026-10-16, sixth round: it is the prologue's lake,
    5.18, reached later by a short forest path from Slimeshire's south-west,
    and each Pearl's homecoming is a short scene there,
    [question 98](story/07-decisions-archive.md#contradictions-left-after-the-review-2026-10-15)*),
    a beaten boss does not appear again (question 6), and the
    Colossus is beaten with his four braziers, not the Hammer
    ([question 59](story/07-decisions-archive.md#contradictions-left-after-the-review-2026-10-15), 12.4).
  - Since the [seventh round](#the-seventh-round-questions-108-to-114)
    (question 110): every Pearl goes home to the Wellspring, which
    brightens, and its power flows into the town: the well comes back with
    Pearl 1, the Forge lights with Pearl 2 (5.17, 8.5).
  - Since the [eighth round](#the-eighth-round-questions-11-to-38-and-115-to-135)
    (questions 17 and 19): a beaten boss also leaves a full heart (+20, 5.3),
    and beating it raises max energy (5.6). The Matron lives at the top of
    the Weaver Hollow (8.14).
    - Build (added): the boss's full heart beside its Pearl, and its
      max-energy raise when it falls.
    - Done when (added): each boss's heart and its max-energy raise come once,

    Eye is beaten; his Pearl grants it once, with the ceremony, and the
    Matron's grants Silk the same way; the skins survive save and load; the
    slime cannot leave a boss chamber mid-fight; a beaten boss never comes
    back. This replaces the Done when below.
  - Changed 2026-10-17 ([the 2026-10-17 decisions](#the-2026-10-17-decisions)):
    **the Pearl is found inside the dungeon, before its boss**, as Zelda's

    his dungeon and keeps it close to draw on its power. Taking it enrages
    him (his strength stays the same), and he seals the dungeon's exits: the
    only way out is to beat him. The dungeon's second half teaches the skin,

    still come when he falls, and the Pearls still go home to the Wellspring.

    - Done when (replaces the one above): on a fresh save the Heavy skin is

      the same way; once a Pearl is taken no door to the outside opens until
      its boss falls; his heart and max-energy raise come once, when he falls;
      the skins survive save and load; a beaten boss never comes back.
- Answered 2026-10-02 ([Open questions](story/07-decisions-archive.md#decisions-by-date)):

  (4.13) and grants the boss's ability for good (the reward type of 5.2).

### [ ] 5.10 — Energy comes back: the slow refill, refills that grow back, the Wellspring

- Decided 2026-10-15 ([the 2026-10-15 decisions](#the-2026-10-15-decisions);
  [question 40](story/07-decisions-archive.md#review-of-the-2026-10-15-direction)):
  "energy can recover but really slow, like 1 per second; in addition we will
  add more ways to recover energy" (owner).
  - Energy refills by itself at 1 a second.
  - Refills that grow back (energy flowers or pots) through the world and in
    the dungeons' rooms, but not inside a boss chamber unless the boss makes
    minions the slime kills.
  - A full refill at the Wellspring and at save points. *Changed 2026-10-16
    (question 62): there are no save points; the full refills come from
    beds, the Wellspring and Slimeshire's well.*
  - The quick Roll stays free, and no puzzle costs more than the starting max
    energy.
  - Before more is built, one chapter's route is played and its energy
    counted.
- Today: the slow refill is built in the rework mode (1 a second,
  `character.player.rework.energyRegenPerSecond`; none while rolling), and so
  are the energy shards (one kill in five drops one, +15,
  `combat.energyShard`) and the free quick roll (`quickRollEnergy` 0). F5
  fills the energy in the playground. The classic game refills 8 a second.
  No refill grows back yet, the Wellspring is not built, and the game has
  no save points: it saves from the pause menu, and beds set where the slime
  wakes.
- Build:
  1. Play one chapter's route in the rework mode, count what each step costs
     and gives back, and write the count under this task.
  2. A refill that grows back: it gives energy, then grows back after a time
     (a value in `game-constants.json`). Placed in the world and in dungeon
     rooms, never in a boss chamber unless that boss makes minions.
  3. Full refills from beds, the Wellspring (Planned: the prologue's lake
     since the sixth round, 5.18) and Slimeshire's well (5.17); there are no
     save points (question 62).
  4. Check every puzzle's energy cost against the starting max energy.
- Not decided yet: flowers or pots (or both), and their numbers (after the
  count). *Which places are save points was answered in the sixth round:
  none (question 62).*
- Since 2026-10-16 ([the third round](#the-third-round-questions-68-to-83);
  [question 69](story/07-decisions-archive.md#contradictions-left-after-the-review-2026-10-15)):
  the Crystal Colossus throws minions down, and killed, they can drop energy
  shards (12.4), so his chamber follows the minion rule above.
- Since 2026-10-16 ([the fourth and fifth rounds](#the-fourth-and-fifth-rounds-questions-84-to-94);
  [question 90](story/07-decisions-archive.md#contradictions-left-after-the-review-2026-10-15)):
  Pearl 1 restores Slimeshire's well when it comes home (5.17), and from
  then on the well refills energy.
  - Build (added): the restored well as an energy refill in Slimeshire. How
    much it gives, and whether it grows back like the other refills, are not
    decided yet.
  - Done when (added): the well refills energy only after Pearl 1 has come
    home.
- Changed 2026-10-16, sixth round
  ([the sixth round](#the-sixth-round-questions-62-to-67-and-95-to-107);
  [questions 98 and 105](story/07-decisions-archive.md#contradictions-left-after-the-review-2026-10-15)):
  - **The well** gives a full refill, as a drink in town, and **beds** also
    restore life (sleeping already heals today, 3.4). This answers how much
    the well gives; whether it grows back like the other refills is still not
    decided.
  - **The Wellspring** is the prologue's lake (5.18), no longer its own map
    east of level-1. After the prologue a short forest path from Slimeshire's
    south-west reaches it, so its full refill stays in reach.
  - **No save points** ([question 62](story/07-decisions-archive.md#contradictions-left-after-the-review-2026-10-15)):
    like Zelda, the slime saves everywhere except inside dungeons (5.15).
    The full energy refills come from beds, the Wellspring and Slimeshire's
    well.
  - Build (replaces the well's line above): the well's drink, a full energy
    refill, once Pearl 1 has come home; a full refill at the Wellspring by
    its forest path; a bed's full refill and its heal.
  - Done when (added; replaces "at the Wellspring and at a save point"
    below): the well's drink fills the energy in full after Pearl 1 comes
    home; a bed fills life and energy; the Wellspring refills in full when
    reached by the forest path.
- Changed 2026-10-16, seventh round
  ([the seventh round](#the-seventh-round-questions-108-to-114);
  questions 110 and 113):
  - **The well's refill grows back:** it refills again after a while, so it
    is not used up. This answers the question left above.
  - **Pearl 1** goes home to the Wellspring, and its power brings the well
    back (5.17); the slime does not take the Pearl to the well.
  - Build (added): the well's refill grows back after a time, like the other
    refills (its time in `game-constants.json`).
  - Done when (added): a used well is empty for its time, then refills in
    full again.
- Changed 2026-10-16, eighth round
  ([the eighth round](#the-eighth-round-questions-11-to-38-and-115-to-135);
  questions 19 and 24):
  - **Drops:** enemies and grass drop energy and health refills, and
    sometimes coins, like Zelda's magic jars and hearts. Today one kill in
    five drops an energy shard (+15, `combat.energyShard`), nothing drops
    health, and the game has no grass to cut.
  - **Max energy** also grows from the well and from each boss (5.6, 5.9).
  - Build (added): enemy drops of energy, health and coins, with their
    chances and amounts in `game-constants.json`; grass that drops the same
    when it is cut, as a placed or painted thing in the worlds.
  - Not decided yet: the chances and amounts, which hits cut grass, and
    what grass looks like beside the War Axe's living, green things (the
    clarity rule). *Answered in the tenth round (below), except the chances
    and amounts.*
  - Done when (added): beaten enemies and cut grass drop energy, health or
    coins at their chances; a drop is picked up by walking over it; a slime
    low on both can always refill from what a camp or a patch of grass drops.
- Changed 2026-10-16, tenth round ([tenth round](#the-tenth-round-questions-18-and-20)): **grass** is cut
  by the Spear and, later, the War Axe, and drops energy, health and
  sometimes coins. Grass tufts never look like the Axe's bushes.
  - Build (added; replaces "grass that drops the same when it is cut"
    above): grass tufts a Spear hit cuts, in a look of their own apart from
    the Axe's bushes, dropping energy, health or coins at their chances.
  - Done when (added): a Spear hit cuts a tuft, which drops at its chances;
    no tuft looks like a bush.
- Depends on: 5.6.
- Player proof: a slime that runs dry waits a little or finds a refill; it is
  never stuck.
- Done when: the count is written here; a slime at 0 energy refills 1 a
  second, gets energy from a refill that then grows back, and refills in full
  at the Wellspring and at a save point; no Release 1 puzzle costs more than
  the starting max energy; the owner accepts the feel.

### [ ] 5.11 — Squash Slam is removed

- Decided 2026-10-15 ([the 2026-10-15 decisions](#the-2026-10-15-decisions);
  [question 45](story/07-decisions-archive.md#review-of-the-2026-10-15-direction)):
  the Slam had no job left once the Goo Bomb breaks cracked ground (7.10). The
  One-Eyed Guardian's reward loses it. Its shake-downs went to the Heavy
  skin's Roll (7.8).

  in the classic game. In the rework mode the improved Slam (35 energy) is a
  box ability whose outer ring bounces enemies in place. In the Stone form it
  breaks cracked ground (7.4).

  the Controls list, the creak hint and the playground's lessons. Saves that
  learned it load without it. Its code goes with the old system (7.11).
- Depends on: 7.10, so the cracked ground still opens.
- Done when: on a fresh save nothing teaches or shows the Slam; the cracked
  clearing opens with the Goo Bomb; a save that learned the Slam still loads.

### [~] 5.13 — Young Burr teaches the Roll

### [ ] 5.14 — Falling slabs and their hazards

- Decided 2026-10-16 ([the 2026-10-16 decisions](#the-2026-10-16-decisions);
  [question 61](story/07-decisions-archive.md#contradictions-left-after-the-review-2026-10-15)):
  loose slabs or bridges that fall too fast to cross walking, but slowly
  enough to cross rolling. They come back after 10 seconds. Under them are
  holes:
  - **lava:** damage over time until the slime walks out by a stair set
    there, so it can try again;
  - **spikes:** normal damage each time its invulnerability ends, until it
    steps off;
  - **the void:** damage, and the slime goes back to the start of the
    bridge.
- Today: none of these exist. The elevation's holes are the nearest piece
  ([ELEVATION.md](godot/ELEVATION.md)).
  *Built 2026-10-17:* the dungeon kit's falling slabs, decks and one hazard
  script for the void, spikes and lava ([DUNGEONS.md "The void, slabs and
  spikes"](godot/DUNGEONS.md#the-void-slabs-and-spikes); 300 ms after a touch,
  back after 10 s; tried in the dungeon lab instead of a playground corner),
  placed in Fatty's Lair: 24 slabs over the void in R2 and R11 and over R7's
  spike pit, a landing wherever a Roll ends ([DUNGEONS.md "Fatty's Lair
  (built)"](godot/DUNGEONS.md#fattys-lair-built)). Left: the owner's feel.
- Build: first the void and the spikes, which Chapter 1 needs (lava waits
  for a fire region, question 70's proposal), with the slab. Their times and
  damage go in `game-constants.json`. Tried in the playground first, in a new
  corner to the east or west.
- Open: the hazards and whether they share one script
  ([question 70](story/07-decisions-archive.md#contradictions-left-after-the-review-2026-10-15));
  when a slab falls and how long a bridge is, so quick rolls in a row cross
  it and walking never does
  ([question 71](story/07-decisions-archive.md#contradictions-left-after-the-review-2026-10-15)).
  *Answered later the same day (below).*
- Changed 2026-10-16, third round
  ([the third round](#the-third-round-questions-68-to-83); questions 70 and
  71): Chapter 1's slab bridges cross the void and spikes, and lava waits for
  a fire region. The slabs, the spikes and the lava share one hazard script,
  and the void works like the elevation's holes. A slab falls a set time
  after the slime touches it, tuned in the playground so a bridge is crossed
  with quick rolls in a row and never by walking.
  - Build (replaces the one above): the slab, the spikes and the void for
    Chapter 1. The slab and the spikes are on the one hazard script that the
    lava will use later; the void reuses the elevation's holes. The slab's
    fall time after a touch, its return after 10 s and the damage go in
    `game-constants.json`. Tried in the playground first, in a new corner to
    the east or west.
  - Done when (added): a bridge longer than one roll is crossed with quick
    rolls in a row, and never by walking.
- Depends on: 7.7 (the Roll).
- Player proof: a bridge that walking cannot cross gives way under a
  walking slime and holds a rolling one.
- Done when: in the playground a walking slime falls through a slab and a
  rolling one crosses; a fallen slab comes back after its time; the void
  hurts and sends the slime back to the bridge's start; the spikes hurt each
  time the invulnerability ends until the slime steps off; the owner accepts
  the feel.

- **Built 2026-10-16** ([PROLOGUE.md](godot/PROLOGUE.md)): both screens on

  against C4 rather than on C4's south-west edge (C4's south-west holds the
  starter camp's yard and is too narrow to seal off; no cavern needed), its
  barred gate opening onto `level-1-c4`'s west edge. New Game starts the
  birth (no HUD until the bubble pops, a dawn light); the path out opens only
  when its light things are gone, with the jingle; nothing is saved while the
  prologue runs (`RunState.saving_blocked_reason`); the forest path leads
  back from Slimeshire to the Wellspring afterwards, which then stands open
  and empty; a homecoming spot is marked by the pool. Not built: the
  Wellspring's campfire and the homecoming scenes (5.17). The world map's
  markers and the g-a opening still need redrawing. Tests:
  `tests/test_prologue.gd`.

  ([the sixth round](#the-sixth-round-questions-62-to-67-and-95-to-107);
  [questions 97 and 98](story/07-decisions-archive.md#contradictions-left-after-the-review-2026-10-15);
  [prologue design](design/2026-10-16-prologue.md);
  [Chapter 1](story/chapters/chapter-1-the-clearing.md), Beat 0): a short,
  closed opening before Chapter 1, about 3 to 5 minutes, in the south between
  chunks B4 and C4, ringed by forest so neither the West village nor
  Slimeshire can be seen from it.
  - **Screen 1, the Wellspring (B4):** at dawn a round pool of glowing green
    goo in a clearing ringed by tall trees, with mist and fireflies. A bubble
    rises and pops, and the slime opens its eyes (the birth). A firefly and
    the pool's wading edge give it things to walk to. The only path out is
    choked with light things (clay pots, a crate, a mossy log end) that the
    Gulp clears; the secret jingle plays as it opens.
  - **Screen 2, the glade (C4's south-west edge):** scattered with pots,
    stones and a crate to throw, and closed by the barred gate (5.22), with
    the path on to Slimeshire.
  - **The Wellspring stays:** after the prologue a short forest path from
    Slimeshire's south-west reaches it, and each Pearl's homecoming is a
    short scene there (5.17). A campfire stands by it (5.17). It is no longer
    its own map east of level-1 (5.9).

### [~] 5.25 — World enemies are placed, not spawned

- Built 2026-10-16, the tests updated but not run yet, waiting for the owner's look
  ([enemy spec §3.3](godot/specs/enemy.md#33-placed-enemies-roadmap-525-not-phasers)):
  - **The node:** `game/scenes/encounters/placed-enemy.tscn`
    (`game/enemy/placed_enemy.gd`) under a world's `Enemies` node, dragged in by
    picture from the Scenery dock's new **Enemies** category: its type (the
    Inspector suggests every enemy type), a wander area (the `area_id` of a
    camp's shared `enemy-wander` world area, else its own circle of
    `wander_radius`) and optional `overrides`. The editor shows the enemy's
    picture, its type and its wander outline.
  - **Spawning:** `EnemyPopulation` spawns every placed enemy once when the
    world is built, on its spot, with the camp territory from its wander
    area (wander, chase within the leash, search, give up, walk home);
    nothing respawns it while the slime stays, and the chunk built again
    (walking back in, a door, a load) brings every one back. The `allowed_types`
    filter covers them. The quest waypoint points at the wander area of the
    nearest standing placed enemy of a kill objective's kinds.
  - **Converted:** the 9 spawn areas of the level-1 and Gloop Forest chunks
    became `enemy-wander` areas (their old stay shapes) with their
    `maxPopulation` of placed enemies (24 in all: level-1 3 + 3 + 3 + 4, Gloop
    Forest 2 + 1 + 2 + 1 + 2), and the crystal caverns' spawn table 5 placed
    enemies in each of its 4 chunks (2 swordsmen, 2 brawlers, 1 archer, in two
    camps sharing a wander rect each; placeholders until Chapter 3 places the
    Caverns' own); Gloop
    Forest's worm table, dead in the whole forest and woken in E4 by the cut
    into chunks, was dropped. The dev worlds and the playground keep their
    spawn areas and `icege` its table, as testbeds (question 121's proposal,
    which the owner took in the [ninth round](#the-ninth-round-questions-32-118-121-123-and-136)).
  - **The chunk tool** (`split_region_chunks.gd`) turns a hand-made world's
    spawn areas and live spawn table into placed enemies when it cuts it (a
    table: at most 5 a chunk, in two camps, `tools/lib/enemy_placement.gd`).
- Decided 2026-10-16 ([the seventh round](#the-seventh-round-questions-108-to-114);
  [World](story/03-world.md#enemies-in-the-world-planned)): enemies in the
  world no longer come from spawn areas.

    map. Each enemy has its own wandering area, as some camps have now.

    come back, as in Zelda.
  - Spawn areas stay only in the playground (the bestiary pens and the
    testbed).
  - Beaten bosses still never come back (5.9).
