## 6. Rebuild Slimeshire: The Workshop

- **2026-09-30 (owner: the Workshop comes too early):** the Workshop is now a
  later upgrade of the workbench, after Chapter 2 at the earliest, once the
  Forge makes iron bars. **The Old Workshop** now needs the
  `chapter-2-complete` flag, so Chapters 1 and 2 never offer it. The ruin
  stays in Slimeshire ("Rebuilding it will take iron from a working Forge."),
  and saves that already restored it keep it. Everything built here (story
  variants, station-aware crafting, restoration sites, recipes unlocked by a
  building) stays, and the Forge (8.5) uses it in Chapter 2. The Workshop's
  return is in the [parking lot](#after-release-1--idea-parking-lot).

Crafting buildings are ruined buildings in Slimeshire. Restoring one turns it
into a station the player uses directly and unlocks its recipes; no NPC runs
it. The placeable
workbench stays as the Chapter 1 field station for tier-1 recipes; portable
recipes stay portable so a fresh save cannot softlock.

- **2026-10-15** ([the 2026-10-15 decisions](#the-2026-10-15-decisions)):
  Release 1 crafts only potions and food ("maybe the vault"). The Forge brews
  the potions until evolutions arrive, and the smith teaches the Hammer shape
  at the Workshop in Chapter 5 or 6, after Release 1
  ([question 51](story/07-decisions-archive.md#contradictions-left-after-the-review-2026-10-15)).
  What the Workshop does in Release 1, and what restores a building once the
  quests go ([question 60](story/07-decisions-archive.md#contradictions-left-after-the-review-2026-10-15)),
  are Open.
- **2026-10-16** ([the 2026-10-16 decisions](#the-2026-10-16-decisions)): a
  building is restored when a Pearl comes home (question 60, 5.17), and the
  vault waits for a later release (question 53). What the Workshop does in
  Release 1 is still Open; the smith comes home once the Colossus falls
  (12.3). In the [third round](#the-third-round-questions-68-to-83) the same
  day: Pearl 2 relights the Forge when it comes home, in place of Rekindle
  the Forge and its ore cost (question 79, 8.5).
- **2026-11-20 (owner;
  [decisions archive](story/07-decisions-archive.md#decisions-by-date)):**
  in Release 1 the Workshop is Pip's. He keeps his dad's Workshop while the
  smith stays at his mine; the owner moved it beside the Forge in C4's
  north-east corner (the red mushroom house took its old place north of the
  plaza). Pearl 2's homecoming raises it (`workshop.restored`), with no
  crafting station (its workbench, the Slam Hammer and the crafted gear are
  unhooked), and Pip carves the Bow's rune there (12.6); Pearl 3's homecoming
  lights the Forge (8.5). His quest, Pip's Workshop, follows the two. Built
  the same day ([quests spec 14.2 and 14.3](godot/specs/quests.md)); not
  playtested.

## 7. Gulp — The Signature Mechanic

The slime eats a Gulp material and takes its form for a while. Each form has a
look and one rule. See
[Gulp](GAME_GUIDELINES.md#signature-mechanic-gulp).

**Controls (decided 2026-09-29): one key is the slime's mouth.** It was W
until 4.10 moved movement to WASD; it is now Q (the `eat` control, with a
short eat clip). No menu ever opens to eat. The task notes below say W
because they describe the game when they were checked.

| Input | Result |
|---|---|
| Tap Q near a Gulp spot (or right-click the spot) | Eat from the world: free, uses nothing from the inventory |
| Tap Q away from a Gulp spot, not in a form | Nothing but a hint: "Hold Q to eat what you carry" (changed 2026-09-30: stray taps turned the slime Heavy far from any rock) |
| Tap Q in a form, away from a Gulp spot | Burp the form away (cancel) |
| Hold Q | Quick wheel of carried Gulp materials; the game keeps running; WASD or the mouse choose, release eats (built in 7.2) |

Eating a different material switches forms; eating the same material at a
Gulp spot resets the timer. **Gulp spots** are world objects (a mossy
boulder, a silk cocoon) that never run out and look different from walk-over
piles, which are collected on contact. Every Gulp puzzle has its spot beside
it, so a puzzle never depends on what the player carries.

**Replaced 2026-10-09:** the table above is the old eating; 7.5 built the essence
Gulp in its place.

**Planned 2026-10-02, not built** ([Direction](story/01-direction.md#decisions-of-2026-10-02-planned-not-built)):
the forms become armors the slime keeps (7.5), webs trap the slime and only the
Silk Armor passes them (7.6), and sprint becomes a mid-game roll that breaks
cracked walls in the Stone Armor (7.7). The table above and tasks 7.0–7.4
describe the game today.

**Since 2026-10-08** Gulp is redesigned around essences instead of armors
([the 2026-10-08 decisions](#the-2026-10-08-decisions),
[Gulp](GAME_GUIDELINES.md#signature-mechanic-gulp)). 7.5–7.7, 8.12 and 8.13
build it.

**Since 2026-10-15** ([the 2026-10-15 decisions](#the-2026-10-15-decisions))
the Gulp only grabs and throws, and Swallow and essences are marked for
deletion. The three skins from the Pearls take the forms' place (7.8), webs
get their new rules (7.9), the Goo Bomb breaks cracked ground (7.10), and once
the skins are built the old system goes and the rework is the only game
(7.11). Since the [third round](#the-third-round-questions-68-to-83) of
2026-10-16 the Feral skin's walls have vines and irregularities (7.8), and
the Swim form's mechanics stay as the later Swim skin's power (7.11). Since
the [fourth and fifth rounds](#the-fourth-and-fifth-rounds-questions-84-to-94)
climbable walls show roots and handholds, the Heavy Roll takes the old dead
and built things in the way (7.8), and the War Axe and Stretch Lash wait for
the next release (7.11).

    in reach puffs.
  - **Holding.** The slime swells up and waddles. It cannot attack or use
    abilities. Q throws what it holds, aimed at the pointer (the facing in
    keys-only); it breaks where it lands, by its material, for damage. Stone
    also stuns, and silk leaves a patch that slows enemies.
  - **Swallow**, a learned ability (`swallow`) on 1, in the
    jump's old HUD place. A held essence becomes its form. With nothing held,
    the current form drops out as a whole essence.
  - **Forms.** One at a time, no timer, no energy. A hit pops the form out as a
    blob the slime can suck back for a moment, and the pop takes the hit's
    side effect. Swallowing another essence pops the old one out. A form and a
    held thing survive doors and saves.
  - **Essences** are items: stone, silk and swim.
    - Today's Gulp spots become placed essences, which come back when the map
      is entered again.
    - Enemy drops fade after about 30 s.
    - Interact puts an essence in the bag, and "Use" in the bag puts it in the
      slime. Walking over one does nothing. Recipes can use them.
  - **Forms:** Stone (today's Heavy), Silk (Sticky) and Swim (today's Frog).
    The frog on its lily pad becomes decoration, and swim essences are placed
    by hand until an aquatic monster drops them.
  - The quick wheel and eating carried materials go away.
  - Keys: Q, and 1 for Swallow (4.10).
- Later: two upgrades that let the slime suck in heavy rocks, like Zelda's
  gloves, each with the full ceremony. What they are and where they are found
  is Open.
- Depends on: 4.13 (Swallow's ceremony).
- Player proof: the slime eats the world and becomes it, and a puzzle's
  essence is something to find.
- Done when:
  - in the playground the slime sucks, throws and swallows, and each form does
    its rule;
  - a hit pops the blob, and the blob can be taken back;
  - a form and a held thing survive a door and a save;
  - a wasted placed essence is back after re-entering the map;
  - the owner accepts the feel.

### [—] 7.6 — Webs trap; only the Silk form passes

- Replaced 2026-10-15 by 7.9
  ([the 2026-10-15 decisions](#the-2026-10-15-decisions)): webs slow the
  slime by 50% instead of trapping it, web walls stop it, and the Silk skin
  replaces the Silk form. Kept for its history.
- Decided 2026-10-02 ([Direction](story/01-direction.md#decisions-of-2026-10-02-planned-not-built),
  "Webs"): a web blocks the path and traps the slime in place. Only the Silk
  Armor passes a web, and passing costs energy; when the energy runs out
  while the armor is passing, the armor comes off at that moment. The
  Matron's special-attack webs work the same way, as do some webs in spider
  camps. A web door closes the Matron's camp.
- Changed 2026-10-08 (7.5): the **Silk form**, from a silk essence, passes
  webs. Forms cost no energy, so the energy rule is gone. Tom's web (8.13) is
  different: a weapon tears it.
- Today: a web holds the slime 0.9 s and pushes it back; the one-minute
  Sticky form passes it and, since the 2026-09-30 feedback (4.9), tears it
  open for good.
- Build: one web rule for world webs, the Matron's volley patches (8.7) and
  the spider-camp webs chosen in 8.9. Without the Silk form the slime is
  trapped in place; in it, the slime passes. Also a web door object for the
  Matron's camp (8.7).
- Not decided: how a trapped slime gets free, and whether a web stays after
  the Silk form passes (it tears open today, a fix made for the timed form).
  Ask the owner before building.
- Depends on: 7.5.
- Done when: in the playground a web traps the slime without the Silk form (in
  the Stone form too) and lets the Silk slime through; a Matron volley patch
  behaves the same.

### [~] 7.7 — The Roll replaces the Dodge

- Built 2026-10-09 and reworked the same day after the owner's feedback ("the
  roll should feel like the Zelda sprint"): a dash like Zelda's Pegasus
  Boots. Waiting for the owner's feel. Try it on 2 with F6 on
  `game/dev/playground.tscn`. It keeps the ability id and input action
  `dodge` (saves, quests and the HUD slot are unchanged) and shows as "Roll";
  Stone Tools still teaches it ("curl up, spin, and roll away! Keep holding it
  and you keep rolling.").
  - **Spin-up:** the slime curls into the ball and spins in place for 260 ms
    (`rollSpinUpMs`), laying two dark skid streaks and kicking dust back like
    screeching tires (cue `RollRev`). It does not move and has no i-frames.
  - **Launch:** it shoots off along one of eight directions: the pointer aim,
    else the held movement keys, else the facing, snapped. 460 px/s
    (`rollLaunchSpeed`) easing to 340 (`rollSpeed`) over 450 ms
    (`rollMinMs`), with 400 ms of i-frames from the launch
    (`rollInvulnerabilityMs`); cue `RollLaunch`.
  - **Straight:** the movement keys do nothing while it rolls. It cannot turn
    or slide sideways (`rollTurnRadPerS` was removed).
  - **Held:** after that it keeps rolling while 2 is held and drains 12 energy
    per second (`rollEnergyPerSecond`; energy does not refill while rolling).
    Letting go or running out of energy stops it.
  - **Bonk:** when a wall, a rock or an enemy stops the ball, it bonks (cue
    `RollBonk`). The screen shakes (220 ms at 0.008, `rollBonkShakeMs`,
    `rollBonkShakeIntensity`) with a 60 ms hit-stop (`rollBonkHitStopMs`).
    Things standing within 140 px of the impact wobble
    (`rollBonkWobbleRadiusPx`). An enemy within 34 px takes 10 damage and 300
    knockback (`rollBonkHitRadiusPx`, `rollBonkDamage`, `rollBonkKnockback`;
    damage 0 turns this off). The slime bounces back off it in the knockback
    pose with an 18 px hop, at 400 px/s easing out over 360 ms, 72 px back:
    at least a tile (owner, 2026-10-09; `rollBounceHopPx`, `rollBounceSpeed`,
    `rollBounceMs`), and then the roll
    ends.
  - A knockback, a death, a door or an opened window ends it at once. The
    next roll can start 250 ms after one ends (`rollCooldownMs`). Holding
    something in the mouth or swimming still blocks it.
  - Everything comes with the lesson (owner, 2026-10-09: once learned, the
    slime learns everything about it). There is no later upgrade. *Changed
    2026-10-16 (question 78): the quick roll is the Roll, and the charged
    roll is a later upgrade (below).*
  - Values in `character.player.movement` of `game-constants.json` (the
    `dodge*` values are gone). Code: `game/player/roll/player_roll.gd` and
    `roll_fx.gd`. Art: the `ball-side` row of slime v2 page 4 (Assets below).
    Sounds: Sound Picker round 5 (the game plays option A until the owner
    picks). Tests: `test_player_movement.gd` (updated for the dash, not run
    yet).
- Open (for the owner): the feel and the numbers; its energy cost (5.6
  decides per-use costs; only the held part drains today); and whether a roll
  in the Stone form breaks cracked walls (not built).
- Changed 2026-10-15 ([the 2026-10-15 decisions](#the-2026-10-15-decisions)):
  the quick Roll stays free (in the rework mode, `quickRollEnergy` 0), and
  only the held roll drains energy. The Stone-form roll is replaced by the
  Heavy skin's Roll (7.8): it pushes enemies away instead of stopping on them,
  and a Roll that hits a wall, a tree and so on shakes things loose. Cracked
  walls are the Goo Bomb's. Open: whether Release 1 still teaches the Roll,
  and where, now that Stone Tools goes with the quests
  ([question 61](story/07-decisions-archive.md#contradictions-left-after-the-review-2026-10-15)).
  *Changed 2026-10-16 (below): no enemy push, and young Burr teaches it.*
- Changed 2026-10-16 ([the 2026-10-16 decisions](#the-2026-10-16-decisions);
  [question 61](story/07-decisions-archive.md#contradictions-left-after-the-review-2026-10-15)):
  - Release 1 teaches the Roll in Chapter 1, before Fatty One Eye: young Burr
    in the West village (5.13). It is the quick roll; the held, charged roll
    (the rework's `roll-charge` upgrade) comes later as an upgrade.
  - The Roll crosses falling slabs that walking cannot (5.14), and gets
    through the burrow's gate that drops shut too fast to walk (5.15).
  - The Heavy skin's Roll (7.8) shakes things loose and breaks the loose
    rocks, rubble heaps and old trunks it hits, with a strong screen shake;
    without the skin a Roll's bonk shakes much less. It does not push enemies
    away: that will be an upgrade later, from another skin.
- Changed 2026-10-16, third round
  ([the third round](#the-third-round-questions-68-to-83);
  [questions 77 and 78](story/07-decisions-archive.md#contradictions-left-after-the-review-2026-10-15)):
  - The quick roll is the Roll, and the charged roll is a later upgrade, like
    the Roll's enemy push. This replaces the 2026-10-09 rule that the Roll
    comes whole with its lesson (above and below).
  - The Heavy Roll breaks only small loose things lying in the way: a loose
    rock, a rubble heap, a rotten trunk across a path (7.8).
- Decided 2026-10-08 ([the 2026-10-08 decisions](#the-2026-10-08-decisions)):
  the Dodge becomes the Roll, on key 2 (owner, 2026-10-09). The slime curls into a ball with a
  swirl, like the roll in the old sheet
  (`slime_normalized.webp`, row 6; in `asset/to-delete/characters/` since 2026-10-17). It first spins in
  place, like tires screeching, then shoots off, and it keeps rolling while
  the key is held. On 2026-10-09 the owner set the rest: it goes straight
  like Zelda's dash and cannot move sideways, hitting a wall shakes things
  and the screen and bounces the slime back, and it comes whole with its
  lesson. *Changed 2026-10-16 (question 78): the charged roll is a later
  upgrade (above).*
- Done 2026-10-08 (in the HUD session, for the owner): Shift no longer sprints,
  and Slime Basics' sprint step and the sprint hint are gone.
- Decided 2026-10-02 ([Direction](story/01-direction.md#decisions-of-2026-10-02-planned-not-built),
  "Sprint"): sprint is not available at the start. An Open Pearl grants it
  mid-game as a powerful milestone, and it is a fast roll. It combines with
  armors: rolling in the Stone Armor breaks cracked walls. Since 2026-10-08
  the Roll takes the Dodge's place instead, and since 2026-10-09 the slime
  learns all of it at once (*replaced 2026-10-16, question 78: the charged
  roll is a later upgrade*).
- Today: the Roll (key 2, taught by Stone Tools) spins up, dashes straight on
  while held and bonks back off walls (built 2026-10-09, above). The old
  dodge's 0.5 s roll is gone; its page-2 `roll-*` rows are still built but no
  longer played.
- Build: the Roll in the Dodge's place (`roll`, key 2, taught where the Dodge
  was): the curled ball, the spin-up in place, the launch, rolling straight
  on while the key is held, and the bonk. It is built and tried in the
  playground first.
- Depends on: 5.6 for its energy cost.
- Done when: on a fresh save Stone Tools teaches the Roll on 2; it spins up,
  dashes straight on while held and bonks back off a wall; the owner accepts
  the feel.

### [~] 7.8 — The three skins replace the forms

- *Built 2026-10-17 (commit 48a7b514):* Heavy, Silk and Feral as described
  below, always on once won and stacking, with their locks, the bag's look
  row, `?skins=` and a playground corner ([SKINS.md](godot/SKINS.md)); waiting
  on the owner's play. The old forms' removal is 7.11.

- Decided 2026-10-15 ([the 2026-10-15 decisions](#the-2026-10-15-decisions);
  [The skins](story/01-direction.md#the-skins);
  [question 41](story/07-decisions-archive.md#review-of-the-2026-10-15-direction)):
  each Pearl grants a skin (5.9), always on once learned, with no key. A skin
  acts in its own places and on its own move: one gate, and one change to a
  move the slime already has. It never slows the slime and stacks with the
  others. "Depth from combos" is dropped. Release 1 has three:

  | Skin | Pearl | Its gate | Its change to a move |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | **Heavy** | 1, Fatty One Eye | Sinks the heavy plates; moves statues, pedestals and blocks | A Roll that hits a wall, a tree and so on shakes things loose, so objects fall, and it breaks small loose things lying in its way: a loose rock, a rubble heap, a rotten trunk across a path (question 77), and old dead or built things that lie or lean in the way: grey leaning old trees, old fences, plank barricades, rotten mine timbers (question 93). Its screen shake is strong; without the skin a Roll's bonk shakes much less. It does not push enemies away (owner, 2026-10-16) |
  | **Silk** | 2, the Matron | Passes web walls at −50%; webs no longer slow it (7.9), and a Roll no longer sticks in them (question 131) | The shapes cut webs, which they cannot do before it: a cut opens a web wall for good and frees things wrapped in silk (7.9, question 55). In Release 1 the Spear, the only shape, does it (question 94) |
  | **Feral** | 3, the Crystal Colossus | Climbs some walls it could not climb before. A climbable wall looks different from the others: it has vines and irregularities (thick roots and rocky handholds since the fourth round, question 86). The player holds W (interact), claws show, the slime climbs up and down, and letting go midway drops it, at no cost (owner, 2026-10-16, [questions 56 and 68](story/07-decisions-archive.md#contradictions-left-after-the-review-2026-10-15)) | The Spear's attacks hit twice as fast |

  Pearl 3's skin was Swim until 2026-10-16, when the owner found that Swim
  does not fit the Colossus; later the same day he chose the Feral skin and
  declined the ideas offered (Prism, Burrow, Echo and Shard). Swim moves to a
  later Pearl, after Release 1, and the deep-water secrets are come-back
  secrets for it; the Swim form goes with the old system, and the swimming
  stays (7.11). The enemy push of the 2026-10-15 plan is a later upgrade from
  another skin, in the parking lot. *Changed 2026-10-16, third round
  (question 75): the Swim form is not dropped either; its scene mechanics
  become this later skin's permanent deep-water power (7.11).*

- **Built 2026-10-17** ([SKINS.md](godot/SKINS.md); tests in
  `godot/tests/test_skins.gd`, not run yet; tried in the playground's Skins corner,
  `?map=playground&spawn=6800,1240`, and with `?skins=heavy,silk,feral` anywhere): the
  three skins as won powers (`SkinState`; saved in the run, an old save's `skin.*` flags
  won once), always on and stacking, granted by each Pearl's `skin_id`, which also makes it
  the everyday look and shows its ceremony line; each skin's look while it works (stony,
  silver, furry with claws) and the bag's Skins row for the everyday look. Heavy: the heavy
  plates, pressure plates and blocks, the Heavy Roll (the `heavy` tag, the strong shake, no
  enemy push) breaking plank barricades, rotten timbers, rubble heaps (no longer the Goo
  Bomb's), and the new loose rock, old trunk and old fence, and shaking a loose prize out of
  a tree. Silk: every web rule on `crosses_webs()`, the Spear's cut. Feral: the held climb up
  and down climbable walls (`object.climb-wall`), the free drop, the claws, the Spear at
  twice the tempo. The Stone and Silk forms no longer count; the forms stay until 7.11.
  **Not built:** the grey leaning old tree (knocked down, as the river bridge, burnt by the
  Lantern), roots painted on a layer, and the owner's verdict on the feel.
- Before 2026-10-17: in the rework mode the Stone form, swallowed from a stone essence,
  sank the heavy plates and moved the pedestal and the statue, and was slower;
  the Silk form passed webs, and the Swim form crosses deep water. The Goo Bomb,
  not the Roll, broke rubble heaps (7.10), and nothing climbed walls.
- Build: the three skins as learned powers the Pearls grant, in the forms'

- Open: how each skin shows while it works
  ([question 34](story/07-decisions-archive.md#skins-shapes-and-abilities-2026-10-15));
  which walls the Feral skin climbs, how the player tells them, the keys

  a beast's skin from a crystal golem

  the playground's bomb rubble
  ([question 72](story/07-decisions-archive.md#contradictions-left-after-the-review-2026-10-15)).
  Answered 2026-10-16: young Burr teaches the Roll in Release 1 (5.13).
  *Answered in the [third round](#the-third-round-questions-68-to-83): a

- Open since the third round: a Heavy Roll that knocks an old tree down into

  ([the fourth and fifth rounds](#the-fourth-and-fifth-rounds-questions-84-to-94);

  - **Heavy:** the Heavy Roll owns old dead or built things that lie or lean

  - Build (replaces the Heavy and Feral parts of the one above): for Heavy,

  - Build (added): a grey leaning old tree by the water that a Heavy Roll

  - Done when (added): a Heavy Roll knocks the tree over the river and the

  - **Looks:** what the Gulp lifts is small and sits loose, with the Gulp's

  - **Silk:** with the skin a Roll no longer sticks in a floor web (7.9).
  - **First locks:** the Heavy skin's is Fatty's Lair's way out (5.15); the

  - Build (added): the leaning tree as a placed object; the old breakable

  - Done when (added): no breakable fence stands on a rim, no leaning tree is

  - Build (added): each skin's teaching rooms in its dungeon's second half

### [ ] 7.9 — Webs slow, web walls stop, and the Silk skin passes and cuts

- Decided 2026-10-15 ([the 2026-10-15 decisions](#the-2026-10-15-decisions);
  [Webs](story/01-direction.md#webs-chapter-2);
  [question 44](story/07-decisions-archive.md#review-of-the-2026-10-15-direction)).

  | | Webs | Web walls |
  |---|---|---|
  | **Without the Silk skin** | −50% speed | Cannot pass |
  | **With the Silk skin** | Full speed | Pass at −50% speed |

    to deal with webs to defeat the Matron (8.7). With the skin a cut opens a
    web wall for good and frees things wrapped in silk: a chest, a key, a

    [question 55](story/07-decisions-archive.md#contradictions-left-after-the-review-2026-10-15)).
  - Tom hangs in a cocoon, not a web, so a weapon tears it before the skin
    (owner, 2026-10-16,
    [question 54](story/07-decisions-archive.md#contradictions-left-after-the-review-2026-10-15); 8.13).
  - No web wall, and no web door, blocks the way to the Matron.
  - The Matron's fight is played with the slowdown before the number is fixed

  - Since 2026-10-16 ([the third round](#the-third-round-questions-68-to-83);

    a maze of web walls on Gloop Forest's east side, which only the Silk skin

  - Since the [fourth and fifth rounds](#the-fourth-and-fifth-rounds-questions-84-to-94)

    a second web maze, which only the Silk skin passes, leads into Gloop

  - Since the [sixth round](#the-sixth-round-questions-62-to-67-and-95-to-107)
    ([question 95](story/07-decisions-archive.md#contradictions-left-after-the-review-2026-10-15)):
    the maze to Tom has webs that slow the slime and cannot be cut before
    the Silk skin (8.13).
- Today: a web holds the slime 0.9 s and pushes it back; the Silk form passes
  it and tears it open for good; there are no web walls; the Matron's volley

  Matron's volley patches follow that rule (no more catch and set-back); web

  are freed by a shape's hit with it. The Silk Gulp form stands in for the
  skin; tried in the playground's Webs corner; tests written, not run. The
  Orb-weaver's laid webs are not built.*

  patches (8.7): half speed without the Silk skin, full speed with it; a web

  `game-constants.json`. Tried in the playground first.

### [ ] 7.10 — The Goo Bomb breaks cracked ground

- Decided 2026-10-15 ([the 2026-10-15 decisions](#the-2026-10-15-decisions);
  [question 45](story/07-decisions-archive.md#review-of-the-2026-10-15-direction)):
  the Goo Bomb breaks cracked ground, and Squash Slam is removed (5.11). It
  replaces 7.4's Heavy slam.
- Today: the Goo Bomb (rework mode; 20 energy, a 2 s cast, a 3 s fuse) blows
  cracked walls and rubble heaps, not cracked ground. Cracked ground breaks
  under a Squash Slam in the Stone form (7.4), under a Hammer impact in the
  Stone form (rework mode) and under a Clod Stomper's slam (8.12).
  *Built 2026-10-17 (the Weaver Hollow's bomb pieces): a blast breaks
  cracked ground too (the jingle; the creak names a blast once the bomb is
  learned), and `object.cracked-floor` is a cracked floor whose hole is a
  one-way fall into another world (door.gd `target_marker`). Tests in
  `test_weaver_bombs.gd`, not run yet. The Slam is not removed yet (5.11).*
- Build: a Goo Bomb blast breaks the cracked ground inside its ellipse and
  opens its sinkhole and door, as the slam did (6.1, 7.4), with the secret
  jingle; the creak hint names the Goo Bomb. The cracked clearing in Gloop
  Forest (8.9) opens with it.
- Open: where the Goo Bomb is learned, and whether it is Chapter 2's key tool
  ([questions 31 and 33](story/07-decisions-archive.md#skins-shapes-and-abilities-2026-10-15)).
  *Answered 2026-10-16, fifth round (question 94): it is Chapter 2's key
  tool. Where it comes from, and Tom's beat, are
  [question 95](story/07-decisions-archive.md#contradictions-left-after-the-review-2026-10-15)
  (Open).* *Answered in the [sixth round](#the-sixth-round-questions-62-to-67-and-95-to-107):
  Tom teaches it, at the end of a maze of secrets and tests (8.13).*
- Done when: in the playground and in the cracked clearing a bomb breaks the
  cracked ground and the sinkhole leads down; the hole stays open after save

- Changed 2026-10-16 ([the 2026-10-16 decisions](#the-2026-10-16-decisions);

  rubble heaps break under a Heavy Roll (7.8), not a Goo Bomb, which keeps
  cracked walls and cracked ground. A bomb caught in another bomb's blast
  still goes off (the chain stays, as built).

  - Open: the playground's bomb rubble: the heap by the map's edge and the

    ([question 72](story/07-decisions-archive.md#contradictions-left-after-the-review-2026-10-15);
    the proposal: the islet's prize sits behind a cracked rock face, and the
    heap waits for a Heavy Roll). *Answered later the same day (below).*

    Roll breaks it.
- Changed 2026-10-16, third round

  [question 72](story/07-decisions-archive.md#contradictions-left-after-the-review-2026-10-15)):

  - Done when (added): a thrown bomb opens the islet's rock face, and the

### [ ] 7.11 — One game: the old system goes

- Decided 2026-10-15 ([the 2026-10-15 decisions](#the-2026-10-15-decisions);
  [question 48](story/07-decisions-archive.md#review-of-the-2026-10-15-direction)):
  once the three skins are built (7.8), the rework becomes the only game. The
  old system goes with all its implementation:
  - the classic mode and its switch;
  - Swallow, essences and the forms (7.5; the Swim form goes, not the
    swimming: changed 2026-10-16, below; *changed again in the third round:
    the Swim form's mechanics stay, as the Swim skin's*);
  - crafted weapons and tools, and their recipes (Milestone 2, 6.4, 8.4, 8.6);
  - harvesting and the resource respawn (5.5);
  - Squash Slam (5.11);
  - the classic number-key abilities (4.10).

  Their art moves to a holding folder for later deletion, kept "until we are
  sure we don't need it": `asset/to-delete/` at the repo root, outside the
  Godot project so it is no longer imported, each file keeping its relative
  path.
- Changed 2026-10-16 ([the 2026-10-16 decisions](#the-2026-10-16-decisions);
  [questions 47, 48 and 75](story/07-decisions-archive.md#contradictions-left-after-the-review-2026-10-15)):
  the swimming stays. This task removes the Swim form and its essence, not
  the swimming: the deep-water code stays for the later Swim skin, and the
  deep-water secrets are come-back secrets for it. What replaces the quests
  that hand out today's tools and abilities is answered (question 60): NPCs
  hand over their finds, and chests hold the rest (5.17).
  - Open: a dev switch in the playground that lends the swimming, so it can
    still be tried and its tests keep running
    ([question 75](story/07-decisions-archive.md#contradictions-left-after-the-review-2026-10-15)).
  - Done when (added): the swimming still works for a slime that is lent it.
- Changed 2026-10-16, third round
  ([the third round](#the-third-round-questions-68-to-83);
  [question 75](story/07-decisions-archive.md#contradictions-left-after-the-review-2026-10-15)):
  the Swim form is not dropped either. This task must not remove the Swim
  form's mechanics: its scene mechanics are refactored into a permanent power
  that works in deep water, the later Swim skin. Only its essence and the
  Swallow that turns it into a form go.
  - Build (added): the Swim form's scene mechanics become that permanent
    power, which nothing in Release 1 grants; the swim essence goes with the
    other essences.
  - Not decided: whether the playground lends that power before the Swim
    skin exists (the dev switch above).
  - Done when (added, in place of the one above): a slime given the
    deep-water power swims as the Swim form does today, with no essence and
    no Swallow; a fresh Release 1 run never has it.
- Changed 2026-10-16, fifth round
  ([the fourth and fifth rounds](#the-fourth-and-fifth-rounds-questions-84-to-94);

- Clean-out wave 1 (2026-10-17): the owner approved a clean-out in three
  waves. This first wave took out what nothing uses, the "sure to go" list of
  the clean-out audit; its cliff parts (the old cliff sheets and kits, the
  front-only look) run as their own job.
  - Art: 27 files (4.8 MB with their `.import` files) moved to
    `asset/to-delete/` under their paths in `godot/asset/`: eight Phaser-era

    (`slime_normalized`) and its Heavy and Sticky skins; the painted
    `cracked-wall-2x1.webp`; and 15 sound takes no scene plays: Fatty's two
    steps, the arrow hit, the ability-ready, cancel, confirm and interact
    takes, and the synth telegraph, error, hotbar switch, three combo notes,

    wall, and the skins' builder `build-gulp-form-skins.py` was removed, so

  - Waiting for the owner: wave 2 (the old weapons, harvesting, crafting

- The cliffs' side walls everywhere (2026-10-17, the owner's approval of the
  same day; side walls approved 2026-10-14,
  [ELEVATION.md](godot/ELEVATION.md#side-walls)): every world draws them, the

    chunks); level-1's chunks and the cliff sides lab already drew side

    gone), so a world made later draws side walls by itself. All 52 caches

  - Fixed on the way: a style painted on the `cliffs` layer along part of a

  - Removed: the Walls, Feet, Lips, Rims, Water, Shadows, RampEnds and

  - Moved: the cliff kits `kit-h2.png` and `kit-h3.png` (24 MB of art-tool

    `asset/Originals/elevation/cliff-kit/`, and the removed hand-painted
    experiment's `256x256-8x8-clif.png` (8.7 MB) to `asset/to-delete/`.
  - Open: with side walls, pressing straight down behind a diamond's back
    face stops at its tip (walking along the face passes), and a straight
    front ramp's cut through a wall has no corner stones.

**Milestone 7 complete when:** the playground has one Heavy and one Sticky
puzzle that a player solves without a hint, and the user has accepted Gulp as
fun. Since 2026-10-08 it also needs the essence Gulp, the web rule and the Roll
(7.5–7.7) accepted in the playground. Since 2026-10-15 it needs the three
skins, the new web rules, the Goo Bomb's cracked ground and one game
(7.8–7.11) in place of the essence Gulp's Swallow and forms and the old web
rule.

    art moves to `asset/to-delete/` with 7.11. The slime-trapped-in-web
    effect gives way to the slowdown (7.9), and the Stone roll's cracked wall
    to the Goo Bomb's cracked walls (built in the rework mode).

## 8. Chapter 2 — Gloop Forest

Chapter 2 turns Gloop Forest from an empty map behind the Verdant Gate into
the second chapter. It absorbs the former milestones 6 (iron), 7 (enemy
materials), 8 (Forge), and 13 (progression pass).

### [~] 8.1 — Write the Chapter 2 outline

- Draft 2026-09-30, waiting on the owner:
  [Chapter 2 outline](design/2026-09-30-chapter-2-outline.md)
  (six quests, places, characters from existing sheets, materials with
  renewable sources, recipes, the Matron built around Sticky, three Gulp
  puzzles, build order, and five questions). The quests are not stubbed in the
  catalog yet: `quests:check` needs their items and recipes, which 8.3–8.6
  add; they become `chapterTwo.ts` as those land.
- Squash Slam (fixed 2026-09-30): it hits every enemy and training dummy
  within its radius through the damage router (30 damage, pushed outwards);
  before, like the lash, it only hit the Shift+8 target dummy. Measured in
  the playground: a worm archer went from 40 to 10.
- Owner's answers 2026-09-30: The Old Workshop becomes required in Chapter 2
  (superseded the same day: the Workshop moved after Chapter 2, see 6);
  Pip and Sunny reuse the existing sheets (new art for further characters);
  the Matron's web-volley design is approved for now. Stretch Lash: option B,
  a tool as well as a hit, so the main line teaches it (the outline has it
  as Q1's reward).
- Stretch Lash built 2026-09-30: an animated goo tendril
  (`effect.player.stretch-lash`, Magnific art packed by
  `scripts/effects/pack-stretch-lash.py`) reaches 180 px along the slime's
  facing. Reworked the same day (owner: a hook, not a weapon): it does no
  damage. A loose pickup it touches flies to the slime; anything solid it
  catches (a tree, rock, post, wall, statue or bell) pulls the slime across
  to it, over water too, landing just short of it on walkable ground; a
  **lash bell post** it catches rings (`object.lash-bell-post`,
  `game.lash-bell`, opens the gates named in Opens Gate ID, Magnific bell
  sound). Enemies ignore it. Try it in the playground's lash yard
  (south-east): walking in teaches the lash (`game.ability-lesson`); from the
  west bank pull the wood and stone piles off the island, hook the bell to
  ring it (the pen to the west opens) and fly onto the island, then hook the
  armor statue on the bank to fly back.

- Build: 5–6 quests in `chapterTwo.ts` form, with givers, objectives,
  rewards, flags, and material sources (resources respawn after 5.5). Assign
  the chapter's abilities: Stretch Lash from a quest, Squash Slam from the boss
  (8.7; since 2026-10-08 Squash Slam comes from Fatty One Eye instead, 5.9). Plan the Gulp puzzles and the boss's use of Sticky form around the
  Gulp prototype's verdict (7.0) and the fresh-eyes playtest notes (4.9).
- Done when: the outline is written, every recipe in the chapter has a
  reachable source, it follows the 7.0 verdict, and `pnpm quests:check` passes
  on the stubbed quests. If Gulp changes later in Milestone 7, update the
  outline before starting 8.7 and 8.9.

### [~] 8.5 — Restore the Forge

- Built 2026-09-30: `object.forge` (`objects/forge.scene.json`, the Workshop
  pattern) is an outdoor smelting furnace in the forge yard, between the home's

  forge house stays the player's home: its door leads to the Slime Home, so it
  could not become the ruin the outline suggested. Until the `forge.restored`

  packed by `scripts/houses/pack-forge.py`) with a restoration site (40
  stone, 20 wood, 6 iron ore) that only a player on the quest
  `rekindle-the-forge` (8.10) can use; anyone else reads "The Forge has gone
  cold…". Restored, it is a `forge` crafting station with Smelt Charcoal (5
  wood → 2 charcoal) and Smelt Iron Bar (2 ore + 1 charcoal → 1 bar), and its
  fire and anvil loops play (the anvil prop no longer clangs while the Forge is
  cold). New item `iron-bar` (icon frame 1, stacks to 99). Played headless
  with the flag set: 10 wood made 4 charcoal, 6 ore made 3 bars, and bars,
  charcoal and the flag survived a reload. The quest (Rekindle the Forge, Pip,
  8.10) was then played headless: the restoration paid 40 stone, 20 wood and 6
  ore through the site and the furnace lit. Open: the owner's acceptance on a
  fresh save.

    [its plan](design/2026-10-17-pearl-mid-dungeon.md)). *Built from the
    plan's proposal the same day (below).*
  - Done when (changed): the user beats her with the Silk skin, won from
    Pearl 2 before her; Pearl 2 is no longer set by her fall.
- Built 2026-10-17, the Silk skin's exam (12.8; [Matron spec](godot/specs/matron.md)
  8.2; not yet played by the owner): Pearl 2 left her camp (it lies in the
  Hollow's Hoard). With the Silk skin, round 1 is as built (her webs no
  longer slow or stick, her spit no longer roots); round 2 spins a **cage of
  web walls** round her shadow (eight, spun again where cut every 5 s): a
  thrown bomb stops in its silk ("A thrown bomb sticks in the silk.") and
  blows out of reach, so the slime cuts in with the Spear or wades in at
  half speed and casts inside; round 3 **webs the floor** round her shadow
  (the Silk slime Rolls through it) with her egg sacs. The Goo Bomb still
  tears her cocoon. Without the skin (a dev run) the built rounds play. She
  shows her rage while the Hollow is sealed (the kit's `EnragedLook`), and
  her fall breaks the seals, C2's east doorway's among them. Open: the
  three-way lash (if built, it pulls but never traps a silk-skinned slime),
  the owner's play and tuning.
- Build: one boss on the Fatty model: a telegraphed signature attack, boss
  bar, arena leash, guarded reward, and persisted defeat. Proposal: the
  **Orb-Weaver Matron** webs the arena, and Sticky form lets you cross her
  webs. She drops a Goo Heart and teaches Squash Slam. Write a short spec
  first.
- Done when: every attack has a telegraph of at least half a second; the
  user beats her with Chapter 2 gear; defeat, save/load, and leaving the arena
  reset or persist her as the spec says; the heart is granted exactly once
  (Squash Slam left her reward on 2026-10-08).

  Slime Home's plaster-and-beam style with the interior ambience, and back.
  Played headless: in, sleep (the respawn point became the hut's bed), out. The
  room was copied from the Slime Home's scene by a script, not furnished in
  Scene Studio; the steps, including the Studio route, are in

  route is untried (no blocker found), and the room reuses the Slime Home's
  layout.

  vault's storage (5.8, [question 12](story/07-decisions-archive.md#progression)).
  *Closed in the eighth round as answered before: the vault comes in a later
  release, and more vaults are decided with it (questions 12 and 38).*
- Build: a hut exterior and furnished interior with a bed, so Chapter 2 has its
  own respawn point. Build the interior with Scene Studio as it is today (this
  replaces the old interior-authoring task 4.3); write the steps in a short
  note in `docs/knowledge/` and fix only real blockers.
- Done when: the hut's doors link both ways, sleeping in its bed sets the
  respawn point, and the room was built without hand-editing JSON (or the
  blockers are listed with their fixes).

  `chapter-2-complete` flag shows an **End of Chapter 2** card

### [~] 8.12 — The Clod Stomper drops the stone essence

- Built 2026-10-09, waiting for the owner: `character.clod-stomper`
  (`game/scripts/clod_stomper.gd`, on the enemy script), a camp enemy with
  90 HP, aggro range 230, speed 70 and knockback resist 0.8.
  - In reach (70 px) it never swings: it crouches 700 ms behind a ring
    telegraph (an ellipse 168×100 at its feet), hops 30 px for 340 ms and
    slams. A slime in the ring takes 26 damage and 300 knockback; the screen
    shakes, the ground cracks, and cracked ground within 90 px of its feet
    (`crack_radius`, plus the cracked ground's own radius) breaks. Then it is dazed 700 ms (the time to hit it) and
    stomps again 1400 ms later. Hits never break a stomp; a rolling slime's
    i-frames dodge it.
  - It drops a stone essence every time (`gulp.essenceDrops`), and 10 coins.
  - Placed: the playground's enemy pen; Gloop Forest camps
    `gloop-clod-stompers-hollow` (2560, 3160), south of the iron hollow's
    plate, and `gloop-clod-stompers-clearing` (1700, 2370), east of the
    cracked clearing. That camp keeps it about 260 px from the cracked ground,
    so it breaks the ground only for a slime that lures it over.
  - Art: `godot/asset/MAPS/enemies/256x256-tile_17x1-clod-stomper.webp`
    (idle, crouch, air, slam, defeated, then since 2026-10-19 a four-step walk
    side, down and up) from Magnific GPT 2.5 stills in
    `asset/Originals/enemies/clod-stomper/`, packed by
    `scripts/characters/pack-clod-stomper.py`. Tests: `test_clod_stomper.gd`.
- Decided 2026-10-08 ([the 2026-10-08 decisions](#the-2026-10-08-decisions)).
  It replaces "Earn the Stone Armor"; the armors were dropped.
- Changed 2026-10-15 ([the 2026-10-15 decisions](#the-2026-10-15-decisions)):
  essences and Squash Slam go (7.11, 5.11), so its stone essence drop goes,
  and with it the reason it was placed: showing what a stone essence and a
  Slam do. What it is for now is not decided yet. Its slam still breaks
  cracked ground near it, which the Goo Bomb opens now (7.10).
- Changed 2026-10-16, sixth round
  ([the sixth round](#the-sixth-round-questions-62-to-67-and-95-to-107);
  [question 64](story/07-decisions-archive.md#contradictions-left-after-the-review-2026-10-15)):
  the Clod Stomper stays as it is, and its slam still breaks cracked ground
  near it. It is an enemy, not a power: one of the bestiary's 29 (12.7).
  Only its stone essence drop goes, with the essences (7.11).
  - Done when (replaces the one below): in the playground and in Gloop
    Forest the Clod Stomper telegraphs, slams and breaks cracked ground near
    it; once 7.11 is done it drops no essence; the owner accepts it.
- **The Clod Stomper** is a squat golem of mud and pebbles, a small Fatty One
  Eye. It jumps in place and slams the ground behind a ring telegraph, and its
  slam cracks weak ground near it. The player sees the enemy do what a stone
  essence plus Squash Slam will let them do. Beaten, it drops a stone essence.
- The slime meets it before A Harder Pick sends it into the iron hollow
  ([Chapter 2](story/chapters/chapter-2-gloop-forest.md), Beat 4), and after
  Tom has taught Swallow (8.13).
- Today: the Clod Stomper is built (above). Its slam plays its own
  `StomperSlam` (Mud stomp, Sound Picker round 5, 2026-10-09) instead of
  SlamImpact. Not done: the owner's acceptance, and its other sounds (it
  reuses SlamWindup, Jump, hit-dull and stone-crumble).
- Build: the enemy (scene, AI on the enemy model, art), its stone essence drop,
  and its camps near the iron hollow and the cracked clearing. It is tried in
  the playground first.
- Depends on: 7.5.
- Done when: in the playground and in Gloop Forest the Clod Stomper
  telegraphs, slams and drops its essence; a slime that swallows the essence
  opens the iron hollow's plate; the owner accepts it.

### [~] 8.13 — Tom in the web: learning Swallow

- Changed 2026-11-19 (plan step A3, not playtested): the quest is retired and Tom no longer teaches
  Swallow. His web hangs in a clearing in E5's south-west woods at the end of a short path off the
  trail; three Spear hits tear it and he teaches the **Goo Bomb** right there with the ceremony
  (`game/scripts/tom_lesson.gd`), then waits at Mossy's camp with his tip. A cracked rock in the
  ledge by his clearing is the Goo Bomb's first lock, a short tunnel back to the trail. The text
  below is the beat as built on 2026-10-09.
- Built 2026-10-09, waiting for the owner: the quest **Tom in the Web**
  (`tom-in-the-web`, in `quests-chapter-2.json`). Mossy gives it after Beyond
  the Verdant Gate; its one stage asks to "Cut Tom out of the web"; Tom takes
  the turn-in and gives 20 coins and Swallow. A Harder Pick now requires it.
  - **Tom** (`tom` in `npc-definitions.json`, `character.tom`, idles in
    place): a round blue jelly slime with a moss-green scarf and a small bottle
    of glowing essence (`godot/asset/characters/authored/npcs/tom.webp`). The
    prompt asked for amber-orange; the blue was kept because it reads well and
    differs from the other NPCs.
  - **The web** (`object.tom-web`, `game/scripts/tom_web.gd`) hangs in Gloop
    Forest's south-west spider thicket at (860, 2470). Three weapon hits tear
    it; thrown things and abilities only shake it; it tears only while the
    quest is active (before that, Tom mumbles). Torn, it sets `tom-freed` and
    Tom calls "I'm free! Thank you! Meet me at Mossy's camp!". Story variants
    then show the torn web and Tom by the camp fire (`camp/tom-at-camp`). The
    quest arrow finds the web.
  - Art: `godot/asset/MAPS/props/256x256-tile_2x1-tom-web.webp` (Tom in the
    cocoon, the torn web); sources in `asset/Originals/characters/tom/`,
    packed by `scripts/characters/pack-tom.py`. Tests: `test_tom.gd`.
- Decided 2026-10-08 ([the 2026-10-08 decisions](#the-2026-10-08-decisions)).
  It replaces "Earn the Silk Armor"; the armors were dropped.
- Changed 2026-10-15 ([the 2026-10-15 decisions](#the-2026-10-15-decisions)):
  Swallow goes (7.11), so Tom has nothing left to teach. Whether he teaches
  Chapter 2's key tool or his beat goes is
  [question 31](story/07-decisions-archive.md#skins-shapes-and-abilities-2026-10-15).
  He hangs in a cocoon, which a weapon tears before the slime has the Silk
  skin (owner, 2026-10-16,
  [question 54](story/07-decisions-archive.md#contradictions-left-after-the-review-2026-10-15)),
  and the quests go
  ([question 60](story/07-decisions-archive.md#contradictions-left-after-the-review-2026-10-15)).
- The story:
  - Mossy asks the slime to rescue **Tom**, a new NPC.
  - Tom already knew how to swallow. He went to a spider thicket in Gloop
    Forest to swallow silk and got caught in its web.
  - The slime frees him by hitting the web around him with a weapon. It cannot
    swallow yet, and a path web would trap it.
  - Back at Mossy's forest camp, Tom teaches **Swallow** with the full
    ceremony.
  - The owner may add more to this story later.
- Spiders drop silk essences (7.5), so silk is no longer a hard-to-get key
  item. Swallow is Chapter 2's key item: the Matron and the web secrets need
  the forms it opens.
- Today: Tom teaches Swallow (built 2026-10-09, above), and learning it plays
  the ability ceremony (4.13, built later the same day). Beyond the Verdant
  Gate no longer teaches it (it was a stand-in). Not done: the owner's
  acceptance; the owner may add more story later.
- Build:
  - Tom: NPC art, and dialogue in `quests-chapter-2.json`.
  - Mossy's quest, after Beyond the Verdant Gate and before A Harder Pick.
  - Tom's web, which weapon hits tear. Since 2026-10-16 it is a cocoon, so
    tearing it needs no Silk skin
    ([question 54](story/07-decisions-archive.md#contradictions-left-after-the-review-2026-10-15)).
  - The Swallow lesson at the camp.
- Open: the Weaver Hollow proposal (Beat 8) no longer earns an armor. Keep it
  as a small dungeon, or drop it. *Closed in the eighth round as answered
  before: it is Chapter 2's dungeon, with the Matron at its top (question 25,
  8.14).*
- Changed 2026-10-16, fifth round

  Tom, his maze's way back to its start: he teaches, the slime blows the
  wall, the secret jingle plays and the loop closes. Tom's cocoon hangs by a
  thread with his face showing, so it reads apart from a silk-wrapped prize
  (a small flat parcel that glints) and from the Cocoon Mimic (it breathes).

    Tom, and the way back leads to the maze's start.

  weapon hits; Tom teaches Swallow at the camp, with the ceremony, before A
  Harder Pick; Swallow survives save and load.

### [~] 8.14 — The Weaver Hollow: Chapter 2's dungeon, the Matron at its top

- Decided 2026-10-16, eighth round
  ([the eighth round](#the-eighth-round-questions-11-to-38-and-115-to-135);
  questions 25 and 133;
  [level design guide](design/2026-10-16-level-design.md#the-weaver-hollow)):
  the Weaver Hollow is Chapter 2's dungeon (question 25, closed as answered
  before), and the Matron lives at its top (question 133). Bosses live in
  their dungeons, so her nest in Gloop Forest's north-east becomes the
  Hollow's entrance.
  - Its tool is the Goo Bomb, taught by Tom before it (8.13): cracked walls
    and cracked floors through the Hollow, and her cocoon at the top (8.7).
  - Its own enemies are the Broodling and the Cocoon Mimic, with the Brood
    Warden as its mini-boss (12.7, rows 20 to 22); the skeletons and the
    Watcher Eye stir in its deep rooms.
  - Its webs and web walls stand uncut, so the Hollow shows the Silk skin's
    locks before the Matron gives it; no web wall blocks the way to her.
  - Its big chest holds a heart quarter (question 113). Open: where its big
    key lies, and whether that quarter is one of Gloop Forest's four
    ([question 116](story/06-open-questions.md#open-after-the-seventh-round-2026-10-16);
    the proposal: a second chest behind the Brood Warden, and one of the
    four).
  - Like every dungeon it cannot be saved inside, and quitting or fainting
    resumes at its entrance with what was done kept (question 109, 5.15).
- Changed 2026-10-17
  ([the 2026-10-17 decisions](#the-2026-10-17-decisions)): **Pearl 2 lies in
  the Hollow, before the Matron**, and taking it seals the Hollow's exits
  until she falls. The Hollow's second half teaches the Silk skin, and her
  fight is its exam, so its webs and web walls before her are no longer only
  shown: after the Pearl they are the skin's lessons. Fainting or quitting
  after the Pearl resumes at the entrance, inside, with the doors to the
  outside sealed. The way out no longer waits for a skin the Matron gives.
  - Build and Done when (changed): the Hollow's rework is 12.8, from its
    [plan](design/2026-10-17-pearl-mid-dungeon.md); until then the built
    Hollow keeps the old order.
- Today: built 2026-10-17 (not yet played by the owner; tests written, not
  run), from [its design](design/2026-10-16-dungeon-weaver-hollow.md), as
  [DUNGEONS.md "The Weaver Hollow (built)"](godot/DUNGEONS.md#the-weaver-hollow-built)
  describes: four floor worlds (`weaver-hollow-trunk`, `-roots`, `-boughs`,
  `-crown`, built by `tools/build_weaver_hollow.gd`) on the shared dungeon
  kit (5.15): the hub with the Crown Door seen on its gallery, the Larder's
  cracks, the Well Room's floor dropping into the Roots, the drop gate and the
  hatch back up, the Boughs' drawbridge, the Mimic's key, the Nursery's
  ambush, the Brood Warden and the Hoard's big key, the gallery's shortcut,
  the Matron in the Crown and the Outer Bough's drop. The open world's nest
  camp is gone: `gloop-forest-f4` is the forecourt, with the Watch Oak and its
  cracked root gate. Open: the owner's playthrough; the big chest's heart
  quarter (a Goo Heart stands in, 5.3); the war journal page and the
  Silkspun Stew recipe (coins and a potion stand in); Mossy's tip at the
  forecourt; quest 6's text, which still describes the old nest fight. The
  big chest holds the big key (the kit's big chest, behind the Brood
  Warden), so the heart quarter is B6's prize.
- Build: the Hollow as a dungeon world behind the nest's entrance, laid out
  by the level design guide's dungeon template (a hub with the boss door in
  sight, two wings, small keys, the big key, the map and a shortcut back),
  with the Goo Bomb's locks in nearly every room, its own enemies and
  mini-boss, its big chest's heart quarter, and the Matron's chamber at the
  top; its way out uses the new Silk skin.
- Depends on: 8.13 (the Goo Bomb), 5.15 (a dungeon's saving and resume),
  12.7 (its enemies), 8.7.
- Player proof: Chapter 2 ends in a dungeon that the bomb opens room by room,
  with its boss at the top.
- Done when: on a fresh save the Hollow opens from the old nest; its main
  path needs only the Spear, the Roll, the Heavy skin, the Gulp and the Goo
  Bomb; its big chest gives a heart quarter once; the Matron is fought at its
  top; quitting inside resumes at its entrance with what was done kept.

**Milestone 8 complete when:** Chapter 2 plays from the Verdant Gate to the end
card, and the gear path wood → stone → iron is complete. Since 2026-10-08 Tom
teaches Swallow and the Clod Stomper drops stone on the way (8.12, 8.13).
Changed 2026-10-15: Release 1 has no gear path (no harvesting and no crafted
gear), and Swallow and the essence drops go. Chapter 2 plays from the Verdant
Gate to the Matron's Pearl and the Silk skin; what follows her is
[question 58](story/07-decisions-archive.md#contradictions-left-after-the-review-2026-10-15)
(*answered 2026-10-16: Chapter 3, up to the Colossus's fall, Milestone 12*).
Since the third round of 2026-10-16 Pearl 2 relights the Forge (8.5), and the
Silk skin passes the web-wall maze into Chapter 3 (8.11), which replaces the
end card. Since the fifth round the Goo Bomb is the chapter's key tool, and
Stretch Lash waits for the next release (8.10, 8.13). Since the sixth round
Tom teaches the Goo Bomb at the end of his maze (8.13), and the Matron's
thread waits in her nest for Burr's Bow (8.7, 12.6). Since the eighth round
Chapter 2 ends in the Weaver Hollow, its dungeon, with the Matron at its top
and a fight that needs the Goo Bomb (8.7, 8.14). Since 2026-10-17 her Pearl
lies in the Hollow before her, its second half teaches the Silk skin, and her
fight is the skin's exam (8.7, 8.14, 12.8). Since 2026-11-20 (owner) Pearl 2
raises Slimeshire's Workshop instead of lighting the Forge (6, 8.5), and the
Matron's thread is the string of Pip's bow rune (12.6).

## 12. Chapter 3 — Crystal Caverns

Added 2026-10-15: Release 1 is Chapters 1 to 3
([the 2026-10-15 decisions](#the-2026-10-15-decisions)). This milestone is
numbered 12 so that no task number changes, and it comes after Milestone 8.
The chapter is a [sketch](story/chapters/chapter-3-crystal-caverns.md): dark
caverns that the Lantern lights, the smith at his mine camp, the Crystal
Colossus and Pearl 3, which grants the Feral skin
([question 56](story/07-decisions-archive.md#contradictions-left-after-the-review-2026-10-15);
7.8). Release 1's content ends when the Colossus falls (12.5). Tasks 12.3 to
12.5 come from the owner's answers of 2026-10-16
([the 2026-10-16 decisions](#the-2026-10-16-decisions)), and 12.6, Burr's
Bow, from their fifth round
([the fourth and fifth rounds](#the-fourth-and-fifth-rounds-questions-84-to-94)),
and 12.7, the bestiary, from their sixth round
([the sixth round](#the-sixth-round-questions-62-to-67-and-95-to-107));
the rest are written with the outline. The
[eighth round](#the-eighth-round-questions-11-to-38-and-115-to-135) changed
12.3 to 12.7: the Bow's crystal lens and first targets, the order of the
Bow and the caverns, the Feral skin's first lock, the Thorn Guard posts at
the borders, and the skeletons' story. The
[2026-10-17 decisions](#the-2026-10-17-decisions) put Pearl 3 inside the
Crystal Mine, before the Colossus, and added 12.8, the three dungeons'
rework.

### [ ] 12.1 — Write the Chapter 3 outline

- Build: turn the sketch into an outline, as 8.1 did for Chapter 2: its maps
  and dungeon, its characters, the Crystal Colossus's fight, Pearl 3 and its
  skin, the secrets that skin opens in the maps already visited, its heart
  quarters, and how it starts from Gloop Forest's east exit.
- Open: where Release 1 ends
  ([question 58](story/07-decisions-archive.md#contradictions-left-after-the-review-2026-10-15));
  how the Colossus is beaten without the Hammer, which comes in Chapter 5 or 6
  ([questions 51 and 59](story/07-decisions-archive.md#contradictions-left-after-the-review-2026-10-15));
  Pearl 3's skin, its gate and its change to a move
  ([question 56](story/07-decisions-archive.md#contradictions-left-after-the-review-2026-10-15));
  where the Lantern is learned
  ([questions 31 and 33](story/07-decisions-archive.md#skins-shapes-and-abilities-2026-10-15));
  whether the smith is found here
  ([question 8](story/07-decisions-archive.md#story)); and what replaces the
  quests ([question 60](story/07-decisions-archive.md#contradictions-left-after-the-review-2026-10-15)).
  *Answered 2026-10-16: Release 1's content ends when the Colossus falls
  (question 58, 12.5); his four braziers beat him (question 59, 12.4); Pearl 3
  grants the Feral skin (question 56, 7.8); the smith, found safe at his mine
  camp, gives the Lantern (question 59, 12.3); the journal and NPC finds
  replace the quests (question 60, 5.17).*
- Open since 2026-10-16: which walls the Feral skin climbs, here and in the
  maps already visited, and the fall
  ([question 68](story/07-decisions-archive.md#contradictions-left-after-the-review-2026-10-15));
  a beast's skin from a crystal golem
  ([question 69](story/07-decisions-archive.md#contradictions-left-after-the-review-2026-10-15));
  what the end-of-Chapter-2 card and the crystal seal become (8.11).
  *Answered in the [third round](#the-third-round-questions-68-to-83): a
  climbable wall has vines and irregularities, the slime climbs up and down,
  and a fall costs nothing (question 68, 7.8); the Colossus climbs out of
  reach and throws minions down (question 69, 12.4); the card and the seal
  go, and a web-wall maze that only the Silk skin passes leads into the
  caverns (question 81, 8.11). Open since then: the climbing vines beside the
  War Axe's vines
  ([question 86](story/07-decisions-archive.md#contradictions-left-after-the-review-2026-10-15)).*
  *Answered in the [fourth and fifth rounds](#the-fourth-and-fifth-rounds-questions-84-to-94):
  climbable walls show thick roots and rocky handholds (question 86, 7.8); a
  hill from the Wilds that only the Feral skin climbs is a second way in
  (question 87, 12.5); Burr gives the Bow in this chapter (question 94,
  12.6). Open: Release 1 with one shape
  ([question 96](story/07-decisions-archive.md#contradictions-left-after-the-review-2026-10-15)).*
  *Answered in the [sixth round](#the-sixth-round-questions-62-to-67-and-95-to-107): the
  Spear is Release 1's only shape (question 96); the outline holds the Bow's
  main-path lock, a far eye switch across the mine's chasm, and no Bow in the
  Colossus's fight (question 102, 12.6).* *Since the
  [eighth round](#the-eighth-round-questions-11-to-38-and-115-to-135): that
  lock is a crystal lens on a post (question 127); the caverns' first half
  never needs the Bow, and the mine camp sends the slime to Burr (question
  134); the Feral skin's first lock is the climb out of the Colossus's arena
  (question 132, 12.4); and every heart quarter the outline places is
  reached with Release 1's powers (question 135).*
- Built 2026-10-17: the chapter's dungeon, the Crystal Mine, from its
  [design](design/2026-10-16-dungeon-crystal-caverns.md): fourteen rooms
  (seven dark), the mine cart and the ore lift, the lens across the chasm,
  the crystal veil, the Dark Maze, the Shardback's Den, the Colossus's arena,
  and outside the smith's camp (G4), the Lookout (H4) and the cave road (G5)
  ([DUNGEONS.md "The Crystal Mine (built)"](godot/DUNGEONS.md#the-crystal-mine-built)).
  The outline itself is still to write; the caverns' other chunks are
  placeholders.
- Changed 2026-10-17
  ([the 2026-10-17 decisions](#the-2026-10-17-decisions)): the outline puts
  Pearl 3 inside the Crystal Mine, before the Colossus, with the mine's second
  half teaching the Feral skin and his fight its exam. The built mine has the
  old order until its rework (12.8).
- Done when: the outline is written, the owner has answered the questions it
  needs, and the chapter's tasks are added to this milestone.

### [ ] 12.2 — Release 1's play time with three chapters

- Decided 2026-10-15: Release 1 is Chapters 1 to 3, so the 45–75 minute
  target, set for two chapters, no longer holds.
- Build: work out how long a first-time player takes for the three chapters,
  and set Release 1's target with the owner.
- Open: the target, where Release 1 ends, and when the time is measured
  ([question 58](story/07-decisions-archive.md#contradictions-left-after-the-review-2026-10-15)).
  *Answered 2026-10-16: its content ends when the Colossus falls (12.5), so
  the time runs from the title screen to his fall. The target and when it is
  measured are still Open.*
- Done when: "Release 1 (v0.1) — The Target" names the three-chapter target,
  and 11.1 times the blind playtest against it.

### [ ] 12.3 — The smith's mine camp and the Lantern

- *Changed 2026-11-20 (owner; 12.9): the slime finds the smith in a mine cave
  on the main road of the Caverns' falling-slab maze, no longer at an open
  camp by the road from Gloop Forest. The mine's gate stands early on that
  road, so the trip to Pip for the Bow is short (owner, the same day); his
  camp moves into his cave, he gives the Smith's Crystal with the Lantern,
  and his mine cart's way is a one-way shortcut back to the gate (owner's
  yes to 12.9's order). The camp built in G4 stands until then.*
- Decided 2026-10-16 ([the 2026-10-16 decisions](#the-2026-10-16-decisions);
  [question 59](story/07-decisions-archive.md#contradictions-left-after-the-review-2026-10-15);
  [Chapter 3](story/chapters/chapter-3-crystal-caverns.md), beat 4): the smith,
  Pip's dad, runs the crystal mine, which the Crystal Colossus has blocked.
  He is not trapped, and there is no rescue: the slime finds him safe at his
  mine camp. He gives it the **Lantern**, the Forge's ember, with the full
  ceremony (4.13), and goes home to Slimeshire once the Colossus falls. Pip's
  story stays relighting the Forge (8.5). He teaches the Hammer later, in
  Chapter 5 or 6 (question 51).
- Today: the Lantern is built in the rework mode, which lends it; nothing
  teaches it. The smith is not built.
  *Built 2026-10-17 ([DUNGEONS.md "Outside"](godot/DUNGEONS.md#outside-the-spiral)):
  the mine camp in G4 (`tools/build_mine_outside.gd`), the smith (four poses,
  Magnific; `game/crystal_mine/smith_actor.gd`) and his talks
  (`mine_camp.gd`): the Lantern once with its ceremony, the gate's brazier as
  its first lock (one flame opens the mine's gate for good), the line that
  sends the slime to Burr; he is gone from the camp once the Colossus fell.
  Not built: the smith in Slimeshire, the burnables beyond the deadthorn
  hedge, the cave road's dark side cave. Tests (not run yet):
  `godot/tests/test_mine_camp.gd`.*
- Changed 2026-11-20 (owner;
  [decisions archive](story/07-decisions-archive.md#decisions-by-date)): the
  smith stays at his mine. Once the gate is open he gives the slime the
  **Smith's Crystal** (`mine-crystal`, flag `mine-camp.crystal-given`) for his
  boy Pip in Slimeshire, who carves the Bow's rune with it (12.6); until the
  Bow he reminds, then gives tips. After the Colossus he stays at the camp
  ("the mine is mine"), proud of Pip. This replaces "goes home to Slimeshire
  once the Colossus falls" above and the line that sends the slime to Burr.
  Built the same day (`mine_camp.gd`); his home in Slimeshire, placed twice
  that day, is gone. Not playtested.
  *Built 2026-11-21 (12.9; [the Spiral](design/2026-11-20-caverns-spiral.md)):
  the camp in G4 is gone (`tools/build_mine_outside.gd` deleted). The smith
  and his camp stand in his cave in the peak's face
  (`crystal-caverns-smith-cave`); his first talk gives the Lantern and the
  Smith's Crystal together, then his lamp's lesson; his lamp frees his ore
  cart (`smith_cart.gd`), a one-way ride down to the mine's gate in G4's
  first yard, where one flame on the brazier opens it. Not playtested;
  `test_mine_camp.gd` rewritten, not run.*
- Build: the mine camp in the Crystal Caverns, the smith (NPC art, dialogue),
  his Lantern gift, and the smith in Slimeshire once the Colossus is beaten.
- Changed 2026-10-16, fourth and fifth rounds
  ([the fourth and fifth rounds](#the-fourth-and-fifth-rounds-questions-84-to-94);
  [questions 84 and 93](story/07-decisions-archive.md#contradictions-left-after-the-review-2026-10-15)):
  the Lantern's **burnables** are dry plant growth (deadthorn, hay, dry roots,
  old banners) and the grey old tree a Heavy Roll knocked down, once it lies
  there as dry wood (an optional combo secret; the main path never needs a

  - Build (added): the burnables beyond today's deadthorn hedge (hay, dry
    roots, old banners), and the fallen old tree as a burnable (with 7.8).
  - Done when (added): each burnable burns under the Lantern's flame and

- Since the [sixth round](#the-sixth-round-questions-62-to-67-and-95-to-107)

### [ ] 12.7 — Release 1's bestiary: 29 enemies

- Decided 2026-10-16, sixth round
  ([the sixth round](#the-sixth-round-questions-62-to-67-and-95-to-107);
  [question 107](story/07-decisions-archive.md#contradictions-left-after-the-review-2026-10-15)):
  the owner accepted the [bestiary](design/2026-10-16-bestiary.md) as
  written. Release 1 has 29 enemies: 10 in Slimeshire Meadow and the Wilds
  (the prologue worm among them), 4 in Fatty's Lair, 5 in Gloop Forest and
  Tom's maze, 3 in the Weaver Hollow and 7 in the Crystal Caverns and the
  mine, with a mini-boss in each dungeon.
  - Each has a tell, can be beaten with the Spear and the Roll, is a one- or
    two-hit job with its right tool, and brings a kind of its own (chaser,
    keeper, turret, burrower, patroller, ambusher, swarm, mimic, shadow,
    sentinel, ...). No two look alike, and a dangerous one never looks like a
    harmless one (the clarity rule).
  - The Gulp never grabs a live enemy; it catches what enemies throw (seeds,
    mud globs, stones) and throws it back.
  - The Clod Stomper stays as it is (question 64, 8.12).
- Today: six are built (the worm swordsman, archer and brawler, the slime
  spider, the orb-weaver and the Clod Stomper, in `enemy-types.json`); five
  were planned in the 2026-10-11 combat plan, and 18 are new. The bosses
  stay as planned.
- Progress 2026-10-16, the first batch: the playground's **Bestiary
  corner** is built (east of the old east edge, the world widened from 52
  to 104 columns; 29 pens in the bestiary's order, each with a sign giving
  the enemy's name, kind and right tool, its enemy back 3 s after it dies,
  F4 for all of them; [BESTIARY.md](godot/BESTIARY.md)). Rows 4 to 10 are
  built with their art (one Magnific pose sheet each), numbers and
  reactions: the prologue worm (one hit point), the Popworm (underground,
  a Heavy Roll's shake throws it out), the Spitcap (its spore seed is the
  first projectile the Gulp catches and throws back), the Hopper, the
  Bramble Boar, the Thorn Guard Statue (a Heavy Roll breaks it) and the
  Rook Thief (steals coins; a thrown pot knocks it down; the Bow's hook is
  in). The six built ones stand in their pens; the other 16 pens say
  "coming next". Not yet: placing them in their regions, and tests.
- Progress 2026-10-16, Fatty's Lair (rows 11 to 14) in pens 11 to 14: the
  Squint (armoured: a ring lights under the slime, it leaps and slams, then
  its visor opens 0.9 s and only the Spear hurts it), the Pipe Worm (a worm
  soldier in a clay pipe in a wall, spitting mud globs on a beat; blows never
  reach it; the glob caught with the Gulp and thrown back kills it, and a
  Roll into the wall shakes it out for the Spear), the Dung Roller (a beetle
  rolling a mud ball along a fixed track, `track`; the ball hurts and clinks,
  the beetle is soft from behind) and the Big Squint (the Squint's script
  with two stomps and a 1.1 s opening). A patterned enemy's kill hit now gets
  through its profile's immune states too.
- Progress 2026-10-16, Gloop Forest and the Weaver Hollow (rows 18 to 22) in
  pens 18 to 22: the Silk Lurker (hangs on a thread over its shadow, drops and
  bites, spins like a pinata when hit; the Bow's hook cuts its thread), the
  Thornball (a hedgehog ball that bounces three times, then lies dizzy; a Goo
  Bomb's blast flips it at once, and a bomb near it makes it cower), the
  Broodling (spiderlings pouring out of an egg sac, six at most; a Goo Bomb
  bursts the sac), the Cocoon Mimic (breathes, unlike the real cocoon beside
  it; grabs the slime, drains its energy until the move keys shake it off; a
  Goo Bomb kills it) and the Brood Warden (two telegraphed sweeps, then
  planted 1.2 s: the only opening). Pens 19 to 21 put the Goo Bomb on F.

  gone), the Glint Bat (asleep on the ceiling, only its eyes glint in the

### [~] 12.8 — The Pearl inside the dungeon: the three dungeons reworked

- *Built 2026-10-17 (commit 48a7b514), waiting on the owner's playthrough and
  [open questions 141 to 145](story/06-open-questions.md#open-after-the-pearl-moved-inside-2026-10-17):*
  the seal kit (`object.dungeon-seal`, the take's roar and glimpse, `EnragedLook`,
  `object.full-heart`, `?sealed`) and all three dungeons reworked as the plan lays
  out ([DUNGEONS.md](godot/DUNGEONS.md#the-seal)). Not built: the boss drums while
  sealed, the journal's Pearl slot, Fatty shrinking; the Mine's second half is
  out of reach in play until the Bow (12.6). The "Today" note below is how it
  stood before the rework.

- Decided 2026-10-17 ([the 2026-10-17 decisions](#the-2026-10-17-decisions);
  [decisions archive](story/07-decisions-archive.md#decisions-by-date)): each
  dungeon's Pearl is found inside it, before its boss, as Zelda's dungeon
  item. A boss cannot swallow his Pearl whole, so he hides it close to draw on
  its power. When the slime takes it the boss notices, enraged (his strength
  stays the same), and seals the dungeon's exits: the only way out is to beat
  him. The dungeon's second half teaches the Pearl's skin, and the boss fight
  is the skin's exam. Fainting or quitting after the Pearl resumes at the
  entrance, inside, with the doors to the outside still sealed until the boss
  falls. The Pearls still go home to the Wellspring afterwards (5.17).
- The plan: [the Pearl mid-dungeon plan](design/2026-10-17-pearl-mid-dungeon.md)
  lays out each dungeon's rework. It supersedes in part the three dungeon
  designs ([Fatty's Lair](design/2026-10-16-dungeon-fattys-lair.md),
  [the Weaver Hollow](design/2026-10-16-dungeon-weaver-hollow.md),
  [the Crystal Mine](design/2026-10-16-dungeon-crystal-caverns.md)) and the
  [level design guide](design/2026-10-16-level-design.md)'s dungeon template.
- Today: the three dungeons were built on 2026-10-17 in the old order
  ([DUNGEONS.md](godot/DUNGEONS.md)): the Pearl (`object.pearl`) shows only
  once its boss is beaten. Fatty's Lair (5.15) holds Pearl 1 in the shrine
  behind Fatty's arena, and the way out is a Heavy Roll through the
  bolt-hole's barricade; the Weaver Hollow (8.14) gives Pearl 2 in the Crown
  after the Matron, and the way out is her web wall on the Outer Bough; the
  Crystal Mine (12.1, 12.4) gives Pearl 3 where the Colossus falls, and the
  way out is the climb up his root wall. The skins themselves are flags that
  stand in until 7.8.
- Build: in each dungeon, as its plan lays it out: the Pearl's hiding place
  before the boss, taken once with its ceremony and its skin; the seal when it

  falls); a second half that teaches the skin; the boss fight as its exam;

- Depends on: 5.15, 8.14 and 12.4 (the built dungeons); 5.9; 7.8 for the

### [ ] 12.9 — A falling-slab maze in the Crystal Caverns

- Decided 2026-11-20 ([the 2026-11-20 decisions](#the-2026-11-20-decisions)):
  a maze built of long falling slabs in Chapter 3, crossed with the Roll.
  Under it is the void or ground that hurts ("the cliff could be void or
  damaged areas", owner).
- Nothing new to build in the kit (owner, the same day: "nothing new, is the
  same we already have for upgrade roll"). A long slab is a run of today's
  one-cell pieces with no landing, as the falling-plank bridge to C5's lake
  island already is: each piece falls 300 ms after a touch and is back after
  10 s, so the run crumbles behind the slime. Only the **Charged Roll** held
  all the way crosses a long run; the quick Roll, quick Rolls in a row and a
  walk fall in. A run meant for the quick Roll has a landing every two pieces
  ([DUNGEONS.md](godot/DUNGEONS.md#falling-planks-over-water)).
- Today: the falling slab (5.14; `object.falling-slab`), the plank and the
  landing (`object.falling-plank`, `object.plank-landing`) and the void and
  spike hazards ([DUNGEONS.md](godot/DUNGEONS.md#the-void-slabs-and-spikes)).
  Fatty's Lair lays slab bridges (R2, R7, R11) and C5 a long plank bridge;
  none is a maze.
- Build: the maze, laid from those pieces over the void or a floor that
  hurts, with branches, dead ends and landings between runs; a fall goes back
  to the last safe ground, as today. Maybe a crystal-rock look for the
  caverns' slabs.
- Decided 2026-11-20 (owner): **the maze is the Crystal Caverns' outside**,
  not a room. It spreads over **several levels of mountains and cliffs**
  (the elevation's levels, stairs and drops), with **islands** the slab runs
  reach and **crystal caves** (rooms with the `crystal-cave` walls) that lead
  on to other parts of the maze, with **monsters and secrets** along the way.
  **The smith is found in one of those mine caves, on the main road.**
- Decided 2026-11-20 (owner): **the mine's gate stands early**, on the main
  road near the way in from Gloop Forest, never at the maze's end. The mine
  needs the Bow (its Chasm), and the Bow means a trip to Pip in Slimeshire
  and back: with the gate early, that trip does not cross the maze again.
  - **The order** (proposed, and taken by the owner the same day: "Yes, that
    order"; the smith across the maze): the slime passes the
    shut gate early and sees where it will go; it crosses the maze to the
    smith's cave on the main road (his camp moves in there: tents, cot,
    campfire, anvil); he gives the Lantern and, in the same visit, the
    Smith's Crystal (it no longer waits on the gate, which is far from him);
    the Lantern's first lock is a lamp in his cave that opens his mine
    cart's way, a one-way shortcut back to the gate; one flame opens the
    gate ("The lamp by the gate" changes to say where); the slime goes to
    Pip for the Bow and comes back to the gate, a short walk from Gloop
    Forest; then the mine, its Chasm and Pearl 3.
  - Also proposed: the caves on the way to the smith are lit (no Lantern
    yet); the maze's dark caves are side ways the Lantern opens on the way
    back or later, with secrets in them; the Feral skin's vines on the
    maze's cliffs hold come-back secrets after Pearl 3. Today's outside (G5
    the cave road, G4 the camp and the mine's gate, H4 the Lookout; H5 still
    the placeholder) is laid again around the maze.
- Decided 2026-11-20 (owner): **long runs stand on the main way too**, so
  Chapter 3's main path needs the Charged Roll, and with it all four Roll
  Fragments. All four can be found before Chapter 3: Burr's in Fatty's Lair,
  Yoi's behind his statue maze (the Heavy skin, Pearl 1), the A4 barn's in
  Glimmerhill, and the Gloop Cavern's under the cracked ground (the Goo Bomb).
